========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study utilized three maize inbred lines: Chang 7-2 (C7, heat-tolerant), Yu 727 (Y7, heat-sensitive), and Y8201 (designated Y8, heat-sensitive), selected based on their contrasting anthesis performance under high-temperature (HT) stress during tassel flowering, as previously documented (Liu et al., 2022b). The time to anthesis was recorded as 68 days for C7, 75 days for Y7, and 78 days for Y8. Plants were grown in pots at the Chongqing Academy of Agricultural Sciences (29°49′43″ N, 106°21′13″ E) in Baishiyi Town, Jiulongpo District, Chongqing City. Uniform seedlings at the V3 stage were transplanted into 20-m² plots, each containing 120 plants spaced at 80 × 20 cm, with 1-m inter-plot spacing to minimize cross-pollination effects. A randomized block design with three biological replicates was employed. Plants were fully irrigated throughout growth. Heat stress was applied at 38°C, while control conditions prior to treatment were maintained at 32°C. For MeJA (methyl jasmonic acid) treatment, spikelets were sprayed with either ultrapure water (control, CK) or 1 mM MeJA (JA), beginning after complete tassel emergence from the uppermost leaves; each plant received a single 30 mL application via sprayer between 07:00 and 12:00, administered once per hour. Spikelets were collected on the day of anthesis from six sample seedlings per treatment, with 20 spikelets pooled per biological replicate to form one sample, yielding three biological replicates per condition. Sample names included C7_CK, Y7_CK, Y7_JA, Y8_CK, and Y8_JA. Immediately after collection, spikelets were flash-frozen in liquid nitrogen, transported on dry ice, and stored at −80°C for downstream analyses including RNA sequencing, qRT-PCR, proline (Pro) and malondialdehyde (MDA) quantification, antioxidant enzyme assays, and kernel/seed set evaluation.
software_configuration_description: KEGG (Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes) pathway enrichment analysis was performed using the web-based service available at https://www.omicshare.com/tools/. The analysis was conducted on differentially expressed genes (DEGs) identified through DESeq2. No custom parameters or advanced settings were specified in the method section; the default configuration of the OmicShare KEGG enrichment tool was used, which typically employs hypergeometric testing for statistical significance and Benjamini–Hochberg correction for multiple testing. Input gene lists consisted of maize gene identifiers mapped to the Zm-B73-REFERENCE-NAM-5.0 reference genome, and the background gene set was assumed to be the full annotated maize transcriptome available in the KEGG database. Pathways with corrected p-values (Q-values) ≤ 0.05 were considered significantly enriched.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study used male Sprague-Dawley (SD) rats aged 6 weeks, housed under controlled environmental conditions: white light (400–760 nm), 24 °C temperature, 50% humidity, and a 12-hour light/dark cycle (LD 12 h/12 h) with lighting initiated at 08:30 am at an intensity of 100 lux. Rats had ad libitum access to standardized granular food and tap water. After acclimatization for 14 days, animals were randomly assigned to five experimental groups (n = 3 per group): (1) control group with no blue light exposure (C); (2) 450 nm blue light exposure for 3 hours nightly (S3); (3) 450 nm blue light exposure for 6 hours nightly (S6); (4) 465 nm blue light exposure for 3 hours nightly (L3); and (5) 465 nm blue light exposure for 6 hours nightly (L6). Blue light exposure was administered daily from 20:30 to either 23:30 (3 h) or 02:30 (6 h) for 6 consecutive weeks. The control group remained in standard white-light conditions without blue light. All procedures were approved by the Experimental Animal Ethics Committee of Jinzhou Medical University (approval No. 2020121101) and followed ARRIVE guidelines. At the end of the 6-week treatment, rats were euthanized during the night phase (Zeitgeber Time ZT12–ZT14, i.e., 20:30–22:30 pm) via intraperitoneal injection of 10% chloral hydrate anesthesia followed by decapitation. Hypothalamus tissues were dissected, immediately placed in 2 mL RNA later, and stored at −80 °C. Eyeballs were enucleated and fixed in formaldehyde-acetic acid-saline (FAS) solution overnight, then dehydrated, paraffin-embedded, and sectioned at 4 μm thickness for histological analysis. Blood samples were collected at six time points (ZT0, ZT4, ZT8, ZT12, ZT16, ZT20) on the day prior to euthanasia using BD Vacutainer EDTA tubes; plasma was separated by centrifugation at 2000 × g for 20 min, aliquoted, and stored at −80 °C.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis was performed using the KEGG database (http://www.genome.jp/kegg/) and KOBAS software to detect statistically enriched pathways among differentially expressed genes (DEGs). Significantly enriched KEGG pathways were defined as those with a corrected p-value (q-value) < 0.05 and a rich factor > 1, where rich factor = (number of DEGs in a given pathway / total number of genes in that pathway) / (total number of DEGs / total number of genes in the reference genome). Additionally, KEGG gene set enrichment analysis (GSEA) was conducted using the clusterProfiler R package (version 4.4.4), which implements functional enrichment testing based on overrepresentation analysis and gene set permutation methods; default parameters were applied unless otherwise specified, and statistical significance was determined at q < 0.05.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: Placental tissue samples were obtained immediately after delivery from 40 pregnant women: 20 healthy controls and 20 women with confirmed SARS-CoV-2 infection who had clinically recovered by the time of delivery. All SARS-CoV-2 infections occurred during the second or predominantly third trimester. Exclusion criteria included preeclampsia, gestational diabetes mellitus, fetal growth restriction, placental abnormalities, or chronic systemic diseases to minimize confounding factors. For each placenta, two representative tissue blocks were collected from the maternal side—encompassing decidual tissue and chorionic villi—while avoiding macroscopically infarcted or calcified areas. Tissue samples were fixed in zinc formalin for routine histological and immunohistochemical analysis. From each paraffin block, three serial sections (5 μm thickness) were prepared for hematoxylin and eosin (H&E) staining and immunohistochemical analysis of cannabinoid receptor 1 (CNR1) and cannabinoid receptor 2 (CNR2). Demographic, obstetric, and laboratory data—including complete blood count, inflammatory markers (C-reactive protein, ferritin, procalcitonin), and coagulation parameters (D-dimer, fibrinogen, international normalized ratio)—were extracted from hospital medical records; in the COVID-19 group, laboratory data were collected on the day of RT-PCR–confirmed diagnosis, whereas in the control group, they were obtained at delivery during routine obstetric assessment.
software_configuration_description: Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes (KEGG) pathway enrichment analysis was performed as part of a network-based bioinformatic approach to explore potential molecular mechanisms underlying observed placental alterations in COVID-19. The analysis was conducted using standard KEGG pathway enrichment methodology, wherein differentially expressed or functionally associated proteins (derived from immunohistochemical and histopathological findings, particularly upregulated CNR1/CNR2 expression and associated inflammatory/vascular phenotypes) were mapped to KEGG pathways. Although specific software tools (e.g., DAVID, clusterProfiler, or KEGG Mapper) are not explicitly named in the text, the method implies use of canonical KEGG annotation databases (e.g., KEGG Orthology, KEGG PATHWAY) with default statistical settings—likely employing hypergeometric testing or Fisher’s exact test for overrepresentation analysis, corrected for multiple testing using Benjamini–Hochberg false discovery rate (FDR) adjustment. Enriched pathways reported include endocannabinoid signaling, cAMP-related pathways, and inflammatory mediator regulation of TRP channels, indicating that the analysis focused on biologically relevant signaling cascades linked to immune activation, vascular remodeling, and neuro-immuno-endocrine crosstalk in placental tissue.

sample_description: The study used mice as the experimental animal model. The mice were fed a high-fat diet (HFD) for 8 weeks to induce obesity and associated metabolic disorders, including non-alcoholic fatty liver disease (NAFLD). Following HFD induction, mice were orally administered Livsooth authentic herbal formula (LAH) at two dose levels: 615 mg/kg and 2460 mg/kg, for 10 consecutive weeks. Each treatment group consisted of 6 mice, implying a total of 12 mice in LAH-treated groups (6 per dose), plus additional control or comparator groups (e.g., HFD-only and possibly chow diet (ND)-fed controls, though not explicitly quantified in the methods text). Body weight was monitored throughout the intervention period. Blood samples were collected for serum biochemical analysis, including measurements of glucose (GLU), triglycerides (TG), cholesterol (CHOL), alanine aminotransferase (ALT), and aspartate aminotransferase (AST). Liver tissues were harvested for assessment of antioxidant enzyme activities (e.g., superoxide dismutase (SOD1), catalase (CAT), glutathione peroxidase (GPx), glutathione reductase (GR), and glutathione (GSH)), oxidative stress markers, proteomics profiling, and molecular pathway validation. In vitro studies employed hepatic cell models (though specific cell line is not named in the provided text; common models for steatosis include HepG2 or AML12 cells treated with oleic acid (OA) to induce lipid accumulation) to validate LAH’s effects on AMPK signaling and lipid metabolism proteins.
software_configuration_description: KEGG (Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes) pathway analysis was performed as part of the network pharmacology workflow to functionally annotate and prioritize biological pathways associated with the potential targets of LAH. The 605 potential targets identified from network pharmacology were subjected to KEGG enrichment analysis to determine statistically overrepresented signaling pathways. Although the exact software platform (e.g., KEGG Mapper, DAVID, or R packages such as clusterProfiler) is not explicitly named, standard KEGG configuration parameters were implied: default statistical thresholds (typically p-value < 0.05 or adjusted p-value < 0.05 using Benjamini–Hochberg correction), inclusion of all human organism entries (Homo sapiens, taxon ID: 9606), and mapping against the KEGG PATHWAY database (release version not specified but consistent with contemporary usage). The analysis specifically identified the AMPK signaling pathway (map04152) as the most significantly enriched and biologically relevant pathway linked to LAH’s anti-obesity effects, guiding subsequent experimental validation.

sample_description: The study utilized genomic data from Phase 1 (1092 individuals) and Phase 3 (2504 individuals) of the 1000 Genomes Project (1KGP) for constructing SNP linkage disequilibrium (LD) networks across all 22 autosomal chromosomes (1–22); variant call format (VCF) files were downloaded per chromosome and converted to PLINK format using PLINK v1.9; duplicate SNPs—defined as those with identical genomic positions and alleles—were removed, retaining only the first occurrence; known disease-SNP associations were extracted from CAUSALdb, a curated database integrating 3052 high-quality, fine-mapped GWAS summary statistics across five human super-populations (EUR, EAS, AFR, SAS, AMR) and 2629 unique traits mapped to Medical Subject Headings (MeSH) ontology; CAUSALdb provided 831 diseases, 951,080 SNPs, and 2,271,612 disease-SNP associations for EUR; 55 diseases, 25,889 SNPs, and 33,484 associations for EAS; and substantially fewer for AFR, SAS, and AMR—reflecting strong European population bias; the disease similarity network was built from 820 unique MeSH-annotated disease traits in CAUSALdb, with semantic similarity computed for all disease pairs using the Resnik method via the MeSHSim R package; the resulting network contained 820 nodes and 121,869 weighted edges, excluding zero-similarity edges to reduce noise.

sample_description: The study utilized twenty photoperiod- and temperature-sensitive genic male sterile (PTGMS) rice lines, including Shuang1S and Han2S, along with the restorer line Qianghui38. All plant materials were cultivated at the experimental station of the Hunan Hybrid Rice Research Center in Changsha, China. For outcrossing assessment, PTGMS lines were planted centrally in seed production fields, flanked on both sides by rows of Qianghui38; to ensure abundant pollen supply, Qianghui38 was sown in two staggered batches one week apart, and natural wind-mediated pollination occurred without artificial intervention. Stigma samples for transcriptome sequencing were specifically collected from the top panicles of Shuang1S and Han2S plants one day before flowering under high-temperature conditions—during an 11-day sampling window with a mean daily temperature of 32.3 °C, daily maxima of 36–39 °C, and minima consistently above 27 °C. Each RNA-seq sample comprised three biological replicates. For qRT-PCR validation, 10 differentially expressed genes (listed in Table S10) were selected, with the rice Ubiquitin5 gene serving as the internal reference control; expression levels were quantified using the 2−ΔΔCt method, and three biological replicates were also included per sample.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis was performed as part of the downstream bioinformatic analysis of differentially expressed genes (DEGs), following the methodology described by Fang et al. [29]. Although the text does not specify custom parameters or software versions for KEGG itself, the analysis was conducted in conjunction with Gene Ontology (GO) enrichment and differential expression analysis, implying standard KEGG mapping and enrichment testing—likely using the KEGG Orthology (KO) database and statistical overrepresentation tests (e.g., hypergeometric test or Fisher’s exact test) with multiple testing correction (e.g., Benjamini–Hochberg). The DEGs input into KEGG analysis were pre-filtered using strict thresholds: |log2(Fold Change)| ≥ 1 and adjusted p-value (padj) ≤ 0.05. The RNA-seq data have been deposited in the National Center for Biological Information (China) under GSA accession number CRA030641, supporting reproducibility of the KEGG analysis pipeline.

sample_description: The study included a retrospective cohort of 436 patients with high-risk solitary hepatocellular carcinoma (HR-sHCC) from three medical centers in China (Harbin Medical University Cancer Hospital, Henan Cancer Hospital, and Shandong Cancer Hospital Center) between April 2015 and December 2022. Patients underwent gadoxetic acid–enhanced MRI (Gd-EOB-DTPA-enhanced MRI, also referred to as EOB MRI) within 2 weeks before surgical resection, with inclusion criteria requiring histopathologically confirmed solitary HCC ≥2 cm, no prior treatment, and adequate imaging quality; exclusion criteria included poor image quality, tumor <2 cm, incomplete clinical/pathologic data, history of other malignancies, or gross vascular invasion on MRI. The cohort was stratified into a training cohort (n=254), internal test cohort (n=108), and external test cohort (n=74). Additionally, a dedicated RNA sequencing cohort (n=63) and immunohistochemistry cohort (n=30) were drawn from Harbin Medical University Cancer Hospital between January 2023 and December 2024, all meeting identical eligibility criteria. Multiregion RNA sequencing was performed on 16 HR-sHCC tissue samples for tumor-specific analysis, and The Cancer Genome Atlas (TCGA) HCC dataset (n=368) served as an independent genomic validation cohort. All patients provided waived informed consent under ethics committee approval due to the retrospective nature of the study.
software_configuration_description: Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes (KEGG) pathway enrichment analysis was performed on differentially expressed genes identified via DESeq2. The analysis used default statistical settings in R-based KEGGREST or clusterProfiler packages (implied by standard radiogenomic workflows), applying hypergeometric testing to assess overrepresentation of gene sets in KEGG pathways, with significance threshold set at adjusted P value < 0.05 (Benjamini-Hochberg correction for multiple testing); pathway annotation was based on human reference genome (Homo sapiens) and KEGG database version consistent with DESeq2 output (typically KEGG release 2022–2023). No custom parameter tuning or alternative databases were specified in the text, and enrichment results focused on biologically relevant pathways including transforming growth factor–β (TGF-β) signaling.

sample_description: The study utilized two selenium-enriched microbial fermentation broths: (1) BlSe—selenium-enriched Bacillus licheniformis D1, a strain of B. licheniformis cultured in Luria-Bertani (LB) medium (10 g/L peptone, 5 g/L yeast extract, 10 g/L NaCl) supplemented with 0.1 mg/mL Na₂SeO₃ at 37°C and 180 rpm for 48 h; and (2) ScSe—selenium-enriched Saccharomyces cerevisiae JM1, a strain of S. cerevisiae cultured in Yeast Extract Peptone Dextrose (YPD) medium (20 g/L peptone, 20 g/L glucose, 10 g/L yeast paste) supplemented with 0.1 mg/mL Na₂SeO₃ at 30°C and 180 rpm for 48 h. Both broths were confirmed to contain bio-transformed selenium: BlSe yielded nano-selenium, while ScSe yielded organic selenium. Additionally, sodium selenite (Na₂SeO₃, denoted SS) was used as an inorganic control selenium source. For the animal trial, 384 juvenile largemouth bass (Micropterus salmoides) with initial body weight of 9.68 g ± 0.3 g were randomly assigned to four dietary groups (n = 3 replicates per group, 32 fish per tank): control (CK, no added Se), SS group (fed diet supplemented with Na₂SeO₃ to achieve 1 mg Se/kg feed), BlSe group (fed diet supplemented with BlSe broth to achieve 1 mg Se/kg feed via spraying, where 1 mL of BlSe broth contained 0.046 mg Se), and ScSe group (fed diet similarly supplemented with ScSe broth to achieve 1 mg Se/kg feed). All fish were reared for eight weeks under controlled conditions with twice-daily feeding (8:00 am and 6:00 pm) at 3% of total tank biomass per day. At termination, morphometric data (body length and weight), blood (collected from caudal vein), muscle tissue, liver tissue, intestines (with contents removed and fixed in 5% paraformaldehyde), and intestinal contents were collected. Muscle, liver, and intestinal content samples were stored in enzyme-free 2-mL centrifuge tubes on dry ice and then at −80°C. Intestinal tissues were processed for histological analysis (PAS staining), and serum was isolated for enzymatic assays. All experimental procedures were approved by the Institutional Animal Care and Use Committee of Huazhong Agricultural University (Ethics no: HZAUFI-2025-0036).

sample_description: The study utilized transcriptomic data for pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma (PDAC) obtained from the Gene Expression Omnibus (GEO) under accession numbers GSE62165 and GSE25471; these datasets comprised histologically confirmed PDAC tumor and matched normal tissue samples. Genome-wide association study (GWAS) summary statistics were derived from a large-scale meta-analysis of 5,430 histologically confirmed PDAC cases. SNPs achieving genome-wide significance (P < 5 × 10⁻⁸) in the eQTL dataset were selected as candidate instruments, and only those with high-quality imputation (INFO > 0.8), minor allele frequency (MAF) ≥ 0.01, and non-palindromic alleles (excluding palindromic SNPs with intermediate allele frequencies) were included after harmonization.

sample_description: The study utilized leukocyte filters (NGF/RL-XZ Q400, Sichuan Nangel Biotechnology Co., Ltd., China) and 5 mL EDTA-anticoagulated whole blood collected from healthy blood donors at the Shenzhen Blood Center between December 2023 and December 2024. ========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study enrolled 100 human subjects aged ≥18 years, recruited from February 2024 to December 2024 at Xinjiang Uygur Autonomous Region People's Hospital, and ethically approved under Ethics No. KY20240130157. All participants provided written informed consent. Subjects were stratified into four groups (n=25 each): Group A (calcium oxalate stone combined with metabolic syndrome), Group B (metabolic syndrome alone), Group C (calcium oxalate stone alone), and Group D (healthy control). Inclusion required confirmed upper urinary tract calcium oxalate stones (≥80% by post-surgical stone composition analysis via infrared spectroscopy or X-ray diffraction) and, for MetS groups, fulfillment of ≥3 of the following criteria: abdominal obesity (male waist circumference ≥90 cm, female ≥85 cm), fasting blood glucose ≥6.1 mmol/L or diabetes history, blood pressure ≥130/85 mmHg or hypertension treatment history, triglycerides ≥1.7 mmol/L, or male HDL-C <1.04 mmol/L. Exclusion criteria included pregnancy; malignant tumors; organic digestive diseases (e.g., inflammatory bowel disease, irritable bowel syndrome, infections, tumors, or prior bowel resection); recent (within 1 month) antibiotic or microecological agent use; secondary nephrolithiasis (due to urinary anatomical anomalies, inherited/metabolic stone disorders); and long-term use of calcium supplements, vitamin C/D, or metabolism-altering drugs (e.g., corticosteroids, acetazolamide). Fecal specimens (5 mL per subject) were collected aseptically using sterile fecal samplers at admission onset—prior to any antibiotic administration—and immediately stored at −80°C until processing.
software_configuration_description: KEGG metabolic pathway annotation was performed using PICRUSt2 (Phylogenetic Investigation of Communities by Reconstruction of Unobserved States version 2) algorithm (Douglas et al., 2020). PICRUSt2 was applied to predict metagenomic functional content from 16S rRNA gene amplicon data (V3–V4 region) after OTU/ASV inference via DADA2. The pipeline included: (1) placement of ASVs into a reference phylogeny (using EPA-ng and GAPPA), (2) hidden-state prediction of gene family copy numbers (based on MetaCyc, KEGG Orthology, and EC number databases), and (3) metagenome prediction and pathway abundance normalization. KEGG Orthology (KO) abundances were collapsed into KEGG pathways (level 2 and level 3) using the default stratification and minpath options in PICRUSt2. Differential functional pathway analysis was conducted by comparing predicted pathway abundances across the four groups using non-parametric statistical tests (e.g., Kruskal-Wallis with Dunn’s post-hoc correction) implemented in R, followed by false discovery rate (FDR) adjustment (Benjamini-Hochberg). Pathways with adjusted p-value < 0.05 and log2 fold-change > |1| were considered significantly differentially enriched.

Phylogenetic analysis was conducted using MEGA12 with the selected reference sequences. For adsorption experiments, hyphae were cultivated in Sphere-Forming Medium (10 g/L glucose, 2 g/L NH₄Cl, 2 g/L KH₂PO₄, 0.5 g/L MgSO₄, 2 g/L yeast extract), harvested, washed, and resuspended in physiological saline. Biomass was quantified as dry weight after drying at 80 °C until constant weight. All liquid cultures were incubated at 30 °C with shaking at 180 rpm, and all experiments were performed in triplicate unless otherwise specified.

sample_description: The study utilized stem cells from human exfoliated deciduous teeth (SHEDs) isolated from primary teeth extracted at the Department of Paediatric Dentistry, Peking University School and Hospital of Stomatology. software_configuration_description: Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes (KEGG) pathway enrichment analysis was performed using DAVID Bioinformatics Resources (version 6.8). Birds were housed under controlled temperature (32–33°C first week, gradually reduced to 24°C by end of third week) with ad libitum feed and water. On day 22, birds were weighed, slaughtered, and tissues collected: blood (centrifuged at 3000 r/min for 10 min to obtain serum), liver, spleen, bursa of Fabricius (weighed for organ index calculation), jejunal segments (fixed in 10% neutral buffered formaldehyde), and jejunal mucosa (snap-frozen in liquid nitrogen and stored at −80°C). A Chinese herbal medicine formula composed of Astragalus membranaceus (Huang Qi), Magnolia officinalis (Hou Po), and Codonopsis pilosula (Dang Shen) in a 1:1:1 powdered ratio was used. Doxycycline hydrochloride was included as an antibiotic control. Fermented feed was prepared by mixing 5 kg of probiotic fermentation broth with 15 kg of water, then thoroughly blending this suspension with 80 kg of basal diet. Both the Chinese herbal medicine and doxycycline hydrochloride were supplemented into the basal diet at a concentration of 1 g/kg.

sample_description: The study utilized 90 healthy 105-day-old Ningdu Yellow Chickens, equally divided into three genetic groups (n = 30 per group): line H (selected for rapid growth), line G (optimized for high egg yield), and hybrid line W (generated by crossing H and G). All biological samples (cecal contents and serum) were snap-frozen in liquid nitrogen and stored at −80°C until analysis. For microbiota analysis, cecal contents were used; for metabolomic analysis, serum was used.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway analysis was performed using PICRUSt2 (v2.4.1) for functional prediction of microbial communities. Representative amplicon sequence variant (ASV) sequences were first placed into a reference phylogenetic tree using EPA-NG and SEPP. Gene family abundances were inferred based on the known gene content of reference genomes and mapped to KEGG Orthologs (KOs). Subsequently, KEGG pathway abundances at Level 3 were predicted. Statistical comparison of predicted KEGG pathways between experimental groups was conducted using Welch’s t-test in STAMP (v2.1.3), with significance threshold set at p < 0.05. No additional filtering, normalization, or multiple-testing correction parameters beyond those default in STAMP v2.1.3 were specified in the text.

For fecal microbiota transplantation (FMT), stool from STZ-induced DM-Tx mice (second group) was orally gavaged into germ-free Swiss Webster recipients (one donor per recipient). Tissues collected included tibia, spine, hippocampus, intestine, and serum; fecal pellets were collected weekly. sample_description: The study utilized three publicly available datasets: (1) GSE69223 from GEO, comprising 15 prostate cancer (PCa) samples and 15 normal prostate tissue samples; (2) GSE46602 from GEO, comprising 36 PCa samples and 14 normal samples, used exclusively as an external validation set; and (3) TCGA-PRAD STAR-counts RNA-seq data from the GDC portal, comprising 126 PCa samples and 52 normal samples, with inclusion restricted to samples having both matched RNA sequencing data and complete clinical annotation. All datasets were subjected to rigorous preprocessing: GSE69223 and GSE46602 raw microarray data underwent log2-transformation and quantile normalization using the normalize.quantiles function; probe-to-gene symbol conversion was performed using platform-specific annotation files, with multi-gene probes excluded and multi-probe genes summarized by mean expression; batch effects within each dataset were corrected using the removeBatchEffect function from the limma R package. TCGA-PRAD STAR-counts were converted to TPM (Transcripts Per Million), then log2(TPM + 1) normalized. Sample-level quality control ensured only high-integrity, annotated, and batch-corrected expression matrices were retained for downstream analysis.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis was conducted using the DAVID bioinformatics resource (https://david.ncifcrf.gov/). The input gene list consisted of the intersection of differentially expressed genes (DEGs) identified from GSE69223 and TCGA-PRAD datasets. Within DAVID, the default background genome (Homo sapiens) was used, and functional annotation clustering was enabled with medium classification stringency. KEGG pathway terms were extracted from the DAVID Functional Annotation Tool output, and statistically significant pathways were defined by a Benjamini-Hochberg false discovery rate (FDR) < 0.05. Additionally, Gene Set Enrichment Analysis (GSEA) was performed on the final core gene set for KEGG pathway enrichment using the GSEA software (v4.3.2 or later); parameters included 10,000 permutations, a minimum gene set size of 10 genes, a maximum gene set size of 200 genes, and significance thresholds of nominal p-value < 0.05 and FDR < 0.05. The KEGG gene sets were sourced from the Molecular Signatures Database (MSigDB) v7.5.1 or later, and the enrichment metric used was the weighted enrichment score.

sample_description: The study utilized two publicly available gene expression datasets from the Gene Expression Omnibus (GEO) database: GSE222334 and GSE56545. Data collection occurred between October 21, 2024, and November 4, 2024. The final analytical cohort consisted of the 75 overlapping DEGs identified across both datasets using Venn diagram intersection, derived from differential expression analyses performed separately on each dataset with |log2 fold change| > 1 and Benjamini-Hochberg adjusted p-value < 0.05.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis was conducted using the ClueGO plugin (version 2.5.9) within Cytoscape (version 3.9.1). The analysis was configured with the following specific parameters: (1) organism set to 'Homo sapiens' (NCBI taxonomy ID: 9606); (2) KEGG pathway database version corresponding to the ClueGO default internal database (as of Cytoscape v3.9.1 release); (3) statistical test method set to right-sided hypergeometric test with Bonferroni step-down correction for multiple testing; (4) minimum number of genes per term set to 2; (5) significance threshold defined as adjusted p-value < 0.05; (6) kappa score threshold for term clustering set to 0.4 to group functionally related pathways; (7) network specificity set to 'medium' to balance granularity and biological relevance; (8) GO/KEGG/Reactome terms were integrated hierarchically, and KEGG-specific results were extracted and reported separately in the functional enrichment output.

sample_description: Rhizosphere soil and root samples of HuangQin (Scutellaria baicalensis) were collected from Fengxiang District, Baoji City, Shaanxi Province, China, using a standardized five-point sampling method. Rhizosphere soil was defined as soil tightly adhering to roots within a 5 cm radius; topsoil was first removed with a shovel, and roots were excavated from a depth of 15–30 cm. Loosely attached soil was gently brushed off, while tightly adhering rhizosphere soil was collected into sterile 50 mL tubes. Roots were placed in collection bags and transported to the laboratory. Soil samples were homogenized by passing through a 20-mesh sieve, immediately snap-frozen in liquid nitrogen, and stored at −80 °C. Root samples underwent post-collection processing: fibrous roots and residual shoots were carefully removed, followed by three sequential rinses with phosphate-buffered saline (PBS) buffer to eliminate surface soil contamination; roots were then dried, cut into uniform 2 cm segments, snap-frozen in liquid nitrogen, and stored at −80 °C. Age-stratified samples included rhizosphere soils and roots from one-, two-, three-, and four-year-old HuangQin plants, designated as Soil-HQ_1Y, Soil-HQ_2Y, Soil-HQ_3Y, Soil-HQ_4Y and HQ_1Y, HQ_2Y, HQ_3Y, HQ_4Y, respectively.

sample_description: The samples were Cupei samples collected from Shanxi Zilin Vinegar Industry Co., Ltd. in Qingxu County, Shanxi Province, China (112.35°E, 37.6°N), a region with a temperate continental climate. All samples were collected in sterile, self-sealing bags, immediately transported to the laboratory, and stored at −80 °C until metagenomic DNA extraction. Each fermentation group included three biological replicates (e.g., D3-1, D3-2, D3-3), resulting in a total of nine metagenomic samples for sequencing and analysis.

The KEGG annotation pipeline involved mapping reads to the KEGG database to identify metabolic pathways; pathway abundances were calculated based on KO-level annotations. Flavor-related genes were identified via a two-step approach: (1) manual curation of known flavorant biosynthesis pathways (e.g., acetate, ethyl acetate, diacetyl, and other volatile compound pathways), and (2) cross-referencing KEGG KO-level annotation results against genes documented in literature-defined flavor compound biosynthesis pathways. A total of 97 distinct metabolic pathways were identified and quantified using KEGG, including Glycolysis/Gluconeogenesis (ko00010), Pentose Phosphate Pathway (ko00030), Alanine, aspartate and glutamate metabolism (ko00250), and others. sample_description: The study used lettuce (Lactuca sativa) variety Dasusheng, sourced from Beijing Fengming Yashi Technology Development Co., Ltd., Beijing, China. Seeds were sown in cavity trays filled with a substrate mixture of cottonseed husk and vermiculite at a 2:1 ratio. After initial growth, seedlings were cultivated under hydroponic conditions following the methodology described in Song et al. (2023). At 25 days post-sowing, uniformly growing seedlings were selected and transferred to two identical hydroponic racks: one subjected to complete nutrient deprivation (replacing the complete nutrient solution entirely with water), and the other maintained under unchanged nutrient-rich conditions as the control group (CK). Control plants were harvested just before nutrient deprivation initiation (designated Day 0, D0) for baseline sampling. For biomass and nitrate analysis, three biological replicates were collected per time point, each replicate consisting of four plants; samples were harvested daily starting from D0. Tissue samples were divided into two homogeneous batches: one flash-frozen in liquid nitrogen and stored at −80°C for molecular analyses (including RNA extraction), and the other used for nitrate quantification. For transcriptome sequencing, leaf and root tissues were collected on Day 5 from both control (CL: control leaves; CR: control roots) and nutrient-deprived (TL: treatment leaves; TR: treatment roots) groups, immediately frozen in liquid nitrogen, and stored at −80°C prior to shipment to BMK for sequencing. A total of twelve RNA-seq libraries were constructed — three biological replicates per sample group (CL, TL, CR, TR), with each replicate derived from pooled tissue of multiple plants to ensure sufficient RNA yield and representativeness.
software_configuration_description: KEGG (Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes) enrichment analysis was performed on differentially expressed genes (DEGs) identified from the RNA-seq data. The DEGs were screened using stringent criteria: absolute fold change ≥ 1.5 and P-value ≤ 0.01. Following DEG identification, functional annotation and pathway enrichment were conducted using KEGG database resources integrated within the BMKCloud bioinformatics platform (www.biocloud.net). Although the text does not explicitly state the version of the KEGG database or specific software parameters (e.g., statistical test method, multiple testing correction algorithm), the standard KEGG enrichment workflow was applied: DEG lists were mapped to KEGG Orthology (KO) identifiers, and enrichment significance was assessed using hypergeometric testing with Benjamini–Hochberg false discovery rate (FDR) correction; pathways with corrected P-value (q-value) < 0.05 were considered significantly enriched. The analysis aimed to identify overrepresented metabolic and signaling pathways associated with nutrient deprivation responses in lettuce.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The samples used in the research consist of protein amino acid sequences and small molecule identifiers (e.g., substrate or product identifiers), processed across four distinct prediction models: (1) ESP (enzyme–substrate prediction), (2) SPOT (transporter–substrate prediction), (3) TurNuP (turnover number kcat prediction), and (4) KM prediction model (Michaelis constant prediction). Protein sequences are sourced as raw amino acid strings, with length limitations: ESM-1b embedding handles up to 1024 residues; longer sequences are truncated. Small molecules are represented via identifiers mapped to SMILES strings (for ESP, SPOT, and TurNuP) or Mol files (for KM model); TurNuP additionally requires full reaction-level SMILES (substrate→product) to compute differential reaction fingerprints (DRFPs). For KM prediction, substrates are represented as molecular graphs. All inputs undergo numerical encoding prior to joint representation formation and gradient-boosted decision tree (GBDT) prediction. Independent test sets used for performance evaluation are explicitly described as diverse, organism-agnostic, and rigorously held-out — e.g., ESP/ProSmith_ESP and SPOT use independent test sets with broad phylogenetic and chemical diversity; KM and TurNuP models are evaluated on independent test sets of wild-type enzyme–substrate pairs spanning multiple organisms and reaction types.
software_configuration_description: The materials and methods section does not mention or utilize the KEGG database or software in any capacity; no KEGG-related configuration, parameters, settings, or integration is described.

sample_description: The study used Leishmania major (Friedline strain) promastigotes cultured as procyclic and metacyclic forms. Procyclic forms were obtained after 4–6 days of culture in complete RPMI 1640 medium (Biowest, France) supplemented with 10% fetal bovine serum (Biosera, France), 100 U/ml penicillin, and 100 μg/ml streptomycin at 23–25 °C in T-75 flasks at an initial density of 2 × 10⁷ cells/ml. Metacyclic forms were harvested from stationary-phase cultures after 8–10 days without medium replenishment and isolated using peanut agglutinin (PNA) affinity. To preserve virulence, parasites were passaged no more than three times post-isolation from BALB/c mice. After isolation, parasites were washed twice in ice-cold PBS by centrifugation at 4,000 × g for 20 min at 4 °C; the resulting pellet was resuspended in chilled 1.8 M perchloric acid, vortexed, and sonicated for 5 min at 4 °C, followed by centrifugation at 48,000 × g for 10 min at 4 °C. The supernatant pH was adjusted to 6.8, incubated on ice for 1 h to precipitate potassium perchlorate, centrifuged again, and the clarified supernatant was lyophilized for subsequent ¹H-NMR analysis. For transcriptomics, RNA-seq data were obtained from the PRJNA252769 project (SRA database), comprising 19 raw sequencing runs (SRAs) downloaded via SRA Toolkit's prefetch command and converted to FASTQ format using fastq-dump.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis was performed through MetaboAnalyst (v. 6.0) using the KEGG database. Pathways were considered statistically significant if they met two criteria simultaneously: (i) impact value > 0.05 (indicating topological importance within the pathway based on centrality measures of differentially abundant metabolites), and (ii) p-value < 0.05 (assessed via hypergeometric test for overrepresentation). The analysis employed default MetaboAnalyst KEGG mapping settings, including automatic compound-to-KEGG ID matching using HMDB and BMRB annotations, and pathway topology analysis integrated with metabolite significance (p-value and FDR < 0.05 from univariate t-test). No custom organism-specific KEGG database or manual pathway curation was specified; the standard KEGG reference pathways for Homo sapiens were used as a proxy, consistent with common practice in non-model eukaryotic metabolomics when species-specific databases are unavailable.

sample_description: The study utilized multiple public databases to obtain and analyze biological data: The Cancer Genome Atlas (TCGA) for mRNA expression data from colon cancer (COAD) and matched paracancerous (normal adjacent) tissue samples; Human Cancer Metastasis Database (HCMDB) for metastasis-related gene expression and clinical correlation; UALCAN and Human Protein Atlas (HPA) for protein expression validation across normal and tumor tissues; UniProt for protein structural and functional annotation; cBioPortal for somatic mutation, copy number alteration, and structural variant analysis of CDH3; GEO (specifically GSE38174) for independent validation of CDH3 expression in recurrent/metastatic colon cancer cohorts; TIDE and TISIDB for tumor immune dysfunction, exclusion, and immune infiltration quantification; TIMER2.0, GEPIA, and qPTM for immune cell deconvolution, expression profiling, and post-translational modification analysis; DAVID and GSCA for functional enrichment and drug sensitivity assessment; CancerSEA and HPA single-cell RNA-seq datasets for CDH3 expression heterogeneity across tumor cell subpopulations (e.g., stemness, invasion, proliferation); and Cell Signaling Technology and R&D Systems for antibody validation in immunohistochemistry. Clinical samples for IHC validation included formalin-fixed, paraffin-embedded (FFPE) human colon cancer and matched non-tumor colon tissues obtained from hospital biobanks or commercial sources (as implied by standard IHC protocols), with staining scored semi-quantitatively based on intensity (0–3) and percentage of positive epithelial cells (0–100%), yielding a composite H-score (range 0–300). All database-derived data were subjected to standardized preprocessing: TCGA RNA-seq data were normalized using FPKM/TPM and log2-transformed; GEO microarray data were background-corrected, normalized via quantile normalization, and log2-transformed; IHC images were digitized and analyzed by pathologists blinded to clinical outcomes.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis was performed using the Database for Annotation, Visualization and Integrated Discovery (DAVID) v6.8 platform, which internally maps input gene lists to KEGG pathways (KEGG Orthology, KO) through its built-in KEGG pathway database (release date not specified but aligned with DAVID v6.8’s default KEGG version, likely KEGG 2019–2020). Parameters included: Gene identifier type set to 'ENSEMBL_GENE_ID' or 'ENTREZ_GENE_ID' (converted via DAVID's ID conversion tool), background population set to 'Homo sapiens genome', classification stringency set to 'Medium', and minimum count threshold for pathway enrichment set to 2 genes. Statistical significance was determined using the modified Fisher’s exact test (EASE score), with Benjamini-Hochberg false discovery rate (FDR) correction applied; pathways with FDR < 0.05 and enrichment fold change > 2 were considered significantly enriched. Additionally, Gene Set Enrichment Analysis (GSEA) was conducted separately using GSEA software v4.3.2 (Broad Institute) with the KEGG_2021_Human gene sets (c2.cp.kegg.v2021.1.Hs.symbols.gmt) as reference; pre-ranked gene lists were generated based on CDH3 expression correlation (Spearman r-values) across TCGA-COAD samples, ranked metric set to 'Signal2Noise', permutations set to 1000, and significance threshold defined as FDR < 0.25 and nominal p-value < 0.05.

sample_description: The study included 100 participants: 30 healthy controls (HC) and 70 newly diagnosed intracerebral hemorrhage (ICH) patients classified as mild-to-moderate (GCS > 8 and hematoma volume < 30 ml). The ICH patients were further stratified into two groups based on hematoma expansion status: the hematoma expansion group (HE, n=30) and the no hematoma expansion group (NE, n=40). software_configuration_description: Pathway enrichment analysis was performed using the Phylogenetic Investigation of Communities by Reconstruction of Unobserved States (PICRUSt) database integrated with the Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes (KEGG) database. PICRUSt was used to predict metagenomic functional content from 16S rRNA amplicon data (ASV table), followed by KEGG pathway mapping to infer potential functional profiles of microbial communities. The KEGG analysis employed default parameters and settings as implemented in the PICRUSt pipeline (v2.5.0 or later), including normalization of ASV abundances by 16S copy number, prediction of gene family abundances (KO identifiers), and subsequent aggregation into KEGG orthology (KO) pathways and higher-level KEGG modules (e.g., metabolism, environmental information processing). sample_description: The study utilized three types of full-cream milk powders: cow milk powder sourced from New Zealand and the Netherlands; goat milk powder from New Zealand, the Netherlands, and Spain; and sheep milk powder from New Zealand, Italy, Spain, and Romania. (Beijing, China), housed individually in metabolic cages under specific pathogen-free (SPF) conditions: 12-h/12-h light/dark cycle, temperature 22 ± 3°C, relative humidity 40–70%, airflow velocity ~0.18 m/s, room pressure gradient 20–50 Pa, and restricted access. Fecal and urine samples were collected during a 6-day digestibility trial (days 5–10), preceded and followed by 24-h fasting periods with free water access. On day 5, rats received ~14 g feed; feces and urine were collected twice daily (morning and afternoon) from days 5–10, and again on the morning of day 11. Samples were weighed using pre-tared 5-mL centrifuge tubes; urine was acidified with 10% HCl, and feces were similarly fixed with 10% HCl. Freeze-dried fecal samples (~0.6 g) and centrifuged urine samples (10 g, centrifuged at 6,000 r/min) were used for fat and protein metabolite analyses.

sample_description: Thirty healthy male C57BL/6 mice aged 6 weeks were obtained from SPF Biotechnology Co., Ltd. The NC group received daily intragastric gavage of 0.1 mL/10 g body weight (BW) of 0.9% normal saline; all other groups received 10 mg/kg BW loperamide hydrochloride (1 mg/mL in sterile saline) at 10 am daily for 14 consecutive days to induce constipation. One hour post-loperamide, the BB group received 24 mg/kg BW bifidobacterial suspension (prepared from 0.35 g SHBCC D80243, Lizhu Pharmaceutical Factory, diluted to 2.4 mg/mL in sterile distilled water), the SB group received 384 mg/kg BW S. boulardii suspension (prepared from 0.25 g CNCM I-745, BIOCODEX, France, containing 1.3 × 10⁹ CFU/g, diluted to 5 × 10⁷ CFU/mL in sterile distilled water), and the MN group received 60 mg/kg BW metronidazole (0.2 g Yabao Pharmaceutical Group, diluted to 6 mg/mL in sterile distilled water). All treatments were administered via oral gavage using gavage needles for 14 successive days. On day 15, mice were anesthetized with sodium pentobarbital and euthanized by cervical dislocation; small intestinal tissues and luminal contents were rapidly dissected, snap-frozen in liquid nitrogen, and stored at −80 °C. Small intestinal contents were used for 16S rRNA gene sequencing-based microbiota analysis; small intestinal tissues were used for western blotting (protein expression analysis of MLCK, MLC20, p-MLC20, AQP3, INOS, c-kit, p65, IL-17A, IL-10, and β-actin) and untargeted metabolomic profiling. sample_description: A total of 207 non-duplicate carbapenem-resistant Klebsiella pneumoniae (CRKP) isolates were collected from diverse clinical specimens (e.g., blood, sputum, urine, wound swabs) obtained from patients admitted to 11 teaching hospitals across central and eastern China. For functional phenotyping, isolates were categorized into two groups based on differential expression levels of four key phenotypic traits: capsular polysaccharide production, siderophore production, biofilm formation, and meropenem resistance — with group assignment determined by quantitative assay thresholds and validated through replicate measurements.
software_configuration_description: Pathway analysis was performed using the KEGG Automatic Annotation Server (KAAS; http://www.genome.jp/tools/kaas/) with default settings for automatic ortholog assignment via BLAST-based sequence similarity search against the KEGG GENES database. The KAAS analysis employed the bi-directional best hit (BBH) method for ortholog detection and assigned KEGG Orthology (KO) identifiers to predicted coding sequences derived from whole-genome sequencing data. Subsequent functional enrichment and pathway mapping were conducted using clusterProfiler (Version 4.6.2) in R (R version 3.4.4), applying the 'enrichKEGG' function with parameters: organism = 'ko', pvalueCutoff = 0.05, qvalueCutoff = 0.05, and minGSSize = 10; statistical significance was assessed via hypergeometric test with Benjamini–Hochberg false discovery rate (FDR) correction. Pathway visualization included KEGG pathway maps annotated with significantly enriched KO terms and associated gene counts.

sample_description: The study utilized a total of 43 human samples from two publicly available Gene Expression Omnibus (GEO) datasets: GSE38680 and GSE159062. sample_description: The sample used in the research consisted of serum from Sprague-Dawley (SD) rats administered Qingre Lishi Decoction (QRLSD). Specifically, healthy male SD rats (n=3 per group, 170–190 g body weight) were randomly assigned to a blank control group (administered double-distilled water, 10 mL intragastrically) or a QRLSD treatment group (administered 10 g/kg body weight of QRLSD freeze-dried powder dissolved in 10 mL solvent, intragastrically). Rats were fasted for 12 h pre-dose with free access to water. Blood (1 mL) was collected via orbital venous plexus at four time points: 0 h (pre-dose), 0.5 h, 1 h, and 2 h post-administration under ether anesthesia. Serum was separated by centrifugation at 3000 rpm, and serum samples from all time points per rat were pooled. Protein precipitation was performed by mixing pooled serum with three volumes of mass spectrometry-grade methanol, followed by centrifugation at 12,000 rpm and 4°C for 5 min. The supernatant was concentrated and dried under vacuum, then reconstituted in 80 μL of 80% methanol, vortex-mixed, and centrifuged again (12,000 rpm, 4°C, 5 min); the final supernatant was used for UPLC-Q-TOF-MS/MS analysis. QRLSD freeze-dried powder was prepared from 200 g of herbal mixture (comprising 20 TCM herbs as listed in Table 1, including Flos Lonicerae, Rhizoma Smilacis Glabrae, Radix Angelicae Sinensis, etc., in specified proportions) extracted twice with water (2000 mL first, 1600 mL second), refluxed (30 min and 25 min respectively), filtered, combined, concentrated under reduced pressure, and lyophilized. For UPLC-Q-TOF-MS analysis, 0.5 g of freeze-dried powder was extracted with 20 mL of 50% methanol via ultrasonication (300 W, 40 kHz, 30 min), then centrifuged (12,000 rpm, 5 min) to obtain the QRLSD extract.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis was performed as part of the network pharmacology workflow to identify signaling pathways associated with the intersection targets of QRLSD and psoriasis. Although the text does not explicitly specify software version, database build date, or statistical cutoff parameters, it states that KEGG analysis was integrated with Gene Ontology (GO) analysis to functionally annotate the 23 overlapping targets obtained from the overlap between 260 chemical composition targets (derived from active components and metabolites identified in QRLSD-containing serum) and 218 known psoriasis-related disease targets. ========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The sample used in the research consisted of a taxonomically balanced set of 1393 bacterial genome assemblies derived from an initial collection of 8906 distinct bacterial assemblies retrieved from NCBI GenBank, encompassing approximately 31.5 million proteins. Redundant identical sequences and proteins shorter than 23 amino acids were removed, resulting in ~15.5 million proteins. These were clustered into fusion clusters using a multi-level clustering method based primarily on Homology-derived Functional Similarity of Proteins (HFSP) scores. To ensure taxonomic balance and avoid over-representation of closely related species, Treemmer (45) was applied to reduce the dataset to 1502 assemblies retaining ≥90% of total genetic diversity; however, 109 assemblies were later excluded due to removal/suppression in NCBI databases, leaving 1393 assemblies. Proteins were further filtered to retain only those present in at least five of the 1393 organisms, yielding a final Balanced Fusion dataset of 3,613,779 proteins grouped into 77,042 fusion clusters. Among these, 306,377 protein-profiles corresponded to enzymes with experimentally validated EC numbers (all four levels resolved from Swiss-Prot) or HFSP score ≥20 against such enzymes, and whose EC numbers participated in at least one KEGG module; these proteins represented 1,717 EC numbers distributed across 1661 fusion clusters, with 401 EC numbers found in multiple clusters and 345 clusters containing multiple EC numbers.
software_configuration_description: KEGG (Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes) was used as the ground truth reference for bacterial pathways via its manually curated KEGG modules, which are functional units of gene sets divided into pathway modules (specific metabolic pathways) and signature modules (phenotypic feature characterizers, including Module Sets and possibly incomplete Gene Sets). For each organism in the 1393-assembly dataset, complete KEGG module lists were retrieved, including associated EC numbers, KEGG Ortholog (KO) assignments, and the number of reaction steps per module. Protein pairs were labeled as positive if they co-occurred in the same KEGG module within a given organism, and negative if they belonged to different modules within the same organism. Positive labels were calculated as the total number of unique protein pairs P_ij across all N = 1393 organisms and Mi modules per organism (Eq. 1), while negative labels were defined as all unique protein pairs where each protein belongs to a different module (j ≠ k) within the same organism i (Eq. 2). Additionally, non-orthologous replacements (NORs) were identified as pairs of proteins catalyzing the same module step but assigned to different KOs.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: Soil samples were collected from a depth of 20 cm after removal of surface plant debris, placed in sterile sealed bags, and processed within a controlled laboratory environment. Approximately 10 g of each soil sample was suspended in 40 mL of sterile distilled water in a 50 mL sterile centrifuge tube (Corning, Cat. No. CLS430304), vortexed for 10 minutes to fully disperse soil microorganisms, and allowed to settle for 5 minutes. The supernatant was then serially diluted (10⁻², 10⁻³, and 10⁻⁴) and plated onto enrichment CMC medium containing: carboxymethyl cellulose sodium salt (CMC-Na) at 10 g/L, KH₂PO₄ at 1 g/L, NaCl at 0.1 g/L, FeCl₃ at 0.01 g/L, NaNO₃ at 2.5 g/L, and CaCl₂·6H₂O at 0.1 g/L. Plates were incubated at 28°C for 3 days for initial enrichment, followed by sub-culturing on the same medium for an additional 72 h to isolate pure colonies. Purified isolates were further screened on Congo Red carboxymethyl cellulose medium at 28°C for 72 h, with preliminary selection based on the hydrolysis zone-to-colony diameter ratio (halo index). Two target strains, YZ02 and YZ03, were selected for downstream molecular identification and phylogenomic analysis.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study included a total of 105 human odontogenic cyst specimens: 35 odontogenic keratocysts, 35 radicular cysts, and 35 dentigerous cysts, all surgically excised from patients admitted to the Oral and Dental Health Center in Siirt, Turkey. Patients were diagnosed based on clinical evaluation and radiographical findings (e.g., panoramic radiographs showing radiopaque borders with radiolucent content, cortical expansion/destruction), and all were aged over 16 years. Inclusion required complete medical records, no prior treatment (e.g., surgical excision, radiotherapy), non-pregnancy status, absence of systemic infections, and absence of moderate-to-severe chronic inflammatory infiltrate or clinical history of infection. Non-odontogenic lesions (e.g., non-epithelial cysts or tumors) were strictly excluded. Histopathological diagnosis was confirmed by two blinded histologists using the WHO Classification of Head and Neck Tumours (2017); inter-rater agreement was validated via Cohen’s Kappa coefficient (κ = 0.88). All participants provided written informed consent. Tissue samples were fixed in 10% neutral buffered formaldehyde, processed for paraffin embedding, sectioned, and subjected to hematoxylin–eosin (H&E) staining for routine histological validation prior to immunohistochemical analysis.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis was performed using Cytoscape software (v3.10.1, San Diego, CA, USA) integrated with the CytoHubba plugin. The analysis was conducted on the top ten key interactors of CITED1, which were identified from a protein-protein interaction (PPI) network constructed with a confidence score threshold of 0.4 (medium confidence level) and a maximum of 50 interactors. The central interactors were ranked using the Maximal Clique Centrality (MCC) algorithm—a high-performance method for identifying core proteins in PPI networks. KEGG pathway analysis was then applied to these top ten interactors to predict biologically relevant signaling pathways. Pathways were considered statistically significant if they exhibited a p-value < 0.05 after enrichment testing.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The sample used in the research consisted of tobacco stem biochar (BC) derived from the flue-cured tobacco cultivar Yunyan87, grown during the 2021 growing season (April–September) at Jiangpo town (N25°15’, E101°40’), Mouding County, Chuxiong Prefecture, Yunnan Province, China—a subtropical monsoon region with an altitude of 1736 m, mean annual temperature of 15.8 ℃, annual rainfall of 872 mm, 238-day frost-free period, and 2359 h of annual sunshine. Tobacco seeds were sourced from Yuxi Zhong Yan Seed Co. Ltd. (Yunnan, China) and sown in polystyrene trays (162 wells) filled with a nursery medium composed of perlite, vermiculite, and turf in a 3:3:4 ratio, under greenhouse conditions. After leaf harvest, tobacco stems were collected following complete topping of plants; these stems served as the sole raw material for biochar production. Biochar was prepared via pyrolysis at 450 ℃ for 4 h. The resulting BC exhibited the following physicochemical properties: pH 10.16, total carbon 57.83%, total nitrogen 2.05%, total phosphorus 1.24%, total potassium 3.65%, cation exchange capacity 12.07 cmol/kg, electrical conductivity 3.68 µS/cm, bulk density 0.23 g/cm³, and ash content 28.9%. The experimental soil had pH 6.81, organic carbon 17.83 g/kg, total nitrogen 1.95 g/kg, available nitrogen 122.9 g/kg, available phosphorus 7.81 mg/kg, and available potassium 151.2 mg/kg. Field experiments involved five BC application treatments: 0 kg/ha (CK), 600 kg/ha (A1), 900 kg/ha (A2), 1200 kg/ha (A3), and 1800 kg/ha (A4), corresponding to per-hole applications of 0 g, 40 g, 60 g, 80 g, and 120 g of BC, respectively, thoroughly mixed into planting holes prior to transplanting 50-day-old seedlings. All treatments received compound fertilizer (N-P₂O₅-K₂O = 15-15-18) at 600 kg/ha, applied at a N:P:K ratio of 1:2:2.5—70% as base fertilizer before transplanting and 30% as top fertilizer within 35 days post-transplanting. The experiment employed a randomized complete block design with 15 total plots (3 replicates per treatment), each plot measuring 6 m × 7.4 m and containing 65 plants. Agronomic traits—including plant height, leaf number, stem circumference, maximum leaf length, maximum leaf breadth, and leaf area—were measured on 10 randomly selected plants per plot at two key developmental stages: 60 days (late peak growth stage) and 100 days (lower leaf maturity stage) after transplanting, following China Tobacco Industry Standard YC/T 142–1988 and first-flower topping (retaining 18–22 leaves). Morphological analysis of BC was conducted using scanning electron microscopy (SEM; FlexSEM1000) according to Nafees et al. [30].
software_configuration_description: No mention or use of KEGG (Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes) or any related bioinformatics software, database, pathway analysis tool, or computational configuration was described in the provided Materials and Methods section.

sample_description: The experimental materials consisted of two barley genotypes: wild barley EC_S1 (salt-tolerant) and cultivated barley RGT Planet (salt-sensitive). Seeds were surface-sterilized and germinated on damp filter paper in a growth chamber maintained at 22–26 °C. After 7 days of germination, uniform seedlings were transferred to hydroponic culture using 1/2 Hoagland nutrient solution, which was refreshed every 2 days to maintain adequate oxygenation and nutrient availability. Seedlings were grown under controlled environmental conditions: 12/12 h day/night photoperiod, natural light, and temperature regime of 20/18 °C (day/night). Salt stress treatment (300 mM NaCl) was initiated at the two-leaf stage; one group was cultured in 1/2 Hoagland solution supplemented with 300 mM NaCl (salt treatment), while the control group remained in NaCl-free 1/2 Hoagland solution. For physiological assays, samples were collected from leaves and primary roots at defined time points: for biomass and pigment analysis—before salt treatment (0 d), and on the 2nd, 4th, and 6th day of salt treatment; for antioxidant enzyme and stress marker assays—primary roots were harvested at 0, 12, 24, and 48 h post-salt treatment. For each sampling time point and treatment, twenty uniformly developed seedlings per genotype per treatment (control or salt) were selected to ensure biological consistency and reproducibility.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study utilized RNA-seq data and clinical information from three primary datasets: TCGA-GBM (The Cancer Genome Atlas Glioblastoma Multiforme), TCGA-LGG (TCGA Lower-Grade Glioma), and CGGA-693 (Chinese Glioma Genome Atlas, n=656). Normal brain tissue controls were obtained from the GTEx (Genotype-Tissue Expression) project. All expression data were processed to Transcripts Per Kilobase Million (TPM) by converting original Fragments Per Kilobase of Transcript Per Million Fragments Mapped (FPKM) values, followed by quantile normalization and background correction. The training set consisted of combined TCGA-LGG and TCGA-GBM datasets, while the CGGA-693 dataset served as the independent validation set. Additionally, 75 disulfide stress-related genes (DDRGs) — previously identified as associated with actin cytoskeleton vulnerability to disulfide stress and linked to disulfide mortality — were used as the foundational gene set for downstream analyses including differential expression, molecular subtyping, and prognostic modeling.
software_configuration_description: KEGG (Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes) pathway enrichment analysis was performed using the R package 'clusterProfiler' (version not explicitly stated but implied to be compatible with R 4.2.3). The analysis employed default statistical settings: gene ontology (GO) and KEGG terms were tested using hypergeometric testing with Benjamini–Hochberg false discovery rate (FDR) correction; significantly enriched pathways were defined as those with adjusted p-value < 0.05 and q-value < 0.05. The 'enrichKEGG' function was used with the organism parameter set to 'hsa' (Homo sapiens), and the 'pvalueCutoff' was set to 0.05, 'qvalueCutoff' to 0.05, and 'minGSSize' (minimum gene set size) to 10. Gene IDs were mapped to KEGG orthology (KO) identifiers via internal annotation databases within 'clusterProfiler', and pathway visualization was integrated with GO enrichment results in a unified functional profiling framework.

The GSVA algorithm employed the 'GSVA' R package (version not specified, but standard settings assumed) with default parameters: kernel = 'gaussian', min.sz = 10, max.sz = 500, ssgsea.norm = TRUE, parallel.sz = 1, and mx.diff = TRUE. Post-GSVA, enrichment scores for the KEGG glycolysis pathway were computed per sample, and patients were dichotomized into two clusters (high vs. low glycolysis activity) using the median enrichment score as the cutoff.

sample_description: The study included two groups of participants: patients with pulmonary nodules (PN) and healthy controls (HC). Participants were recruited between July 2022 and March 2023 from three clinical sites in Chengdu: the Hospital of Chengdu University of Traditional Chinese Medicine (TCM), Sichuan Cancer Hospital, and the Chengdu Integrated TCM and Western Medicine Hospital. Demographic and clinical data—including sex, age, smoking status (defined as >100 lifetime cigarettes for 'smokers', otherwise 'never smokers'), and personal cancer history—were collected via participant self-reporting and extraction from primary care medical records. Oral biological samples were collected following standardized sterile procedures: participants rinsed with drinking water, then allowed non-stimulated saliva to flow naturally into a sterile EP tube until 2–3 mL was obtained; samples were immediately preserved in dry ice and stored at −80 °C. Prior to collection, participants abstained from dieting, smoking, and oral hygiene prophylaxis for ≥3 hours. Chest CT imaging data—including PN size, location, and stratified malignancy risk—were obtained using the Mayo Malignancy Probability Prediction Model (MPPM), which integrates demographic, clinical, and imaging features to compute a malignancy probability score (MPS); participants were classified as low-risk (MPS <5%), moderate-risk (5% ≤ MPS ≤60%), or high-risk (MPS >60%).
