========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study used two main datasets: (1) GTEx v8, comprising 9662 human tissue samples across multiple tissues (with brain tissues grouped as one tissue), from which 7729 samples were allocated for training and 1933 for validation, preserving tissue-wise 80:20 stratification; gene expression count data were obtained via recount2 R package, transformed using Variance Stabilizing Transformation (VST) from DESeq2, filtered to 19,423 protein-coding genes based on GENCODE v33 annotation, and standardized per gene (mean = 0, standard deviation = 1); (2) TCGA project data, including 11,093 RNA-seq samples (8874 for training, 2219 for validation), downloaded via TCGAbiolinks, batch-corrected using ComBat-seq (adjusting for sequencing center while protecting project ID and sample type: tumor vs normal), VST-transformed, filtered to the same 19,423 genes, and gene-level standardized identically to GTEx.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathways were integrated alongside Gene Ontology (GO) biological processes in the NetActivity framework. KEGG terms were manually filtered to exclude collections lacking functional coherence (e.g., hsa02010, ABC transporter collection) or terms representing multiple independent pathways (e.g., hsa04742). Only KEGG pathways containing >10 genes were retained initially, yielding 188 manually curated KEGG pathways. Subsequent filtering removed KEGG (and GO) gene sets with >30 genes and those sharing >50% of genes with other gene sets to improve replicability. The final KEGG configuration comprised 33 non-redundant, functionally coherent KEGG pathways (listed in Supplementary Table S4), embedded within a total gene set space of 1518 (1485 GO + 33 KEGG), each mapped to its constituent genes with strict connectivity constraints in the encoder: neurons in additional encoder hidden layers were only connected to genes within their corresponding gene set, and all neurons representing the same gene set in an added encoder layer projected to a single gene set activity score (GSAS) neuron in the encoder output.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study utilized RNA-seq gene expression data and clinical-pathological information from 594 lung adenocarcinoma (LUAD) samples obtained from The Cancer Genome Atlas (TCGA) database, including 59 normal tissues and 535 tumor tissues; data were downloaded in Level-3 HTSeq-FPKM format and converted to transcripts per million (TPM) for normalization. Samples were stratified into TNFSF11 high-expression (top 50%, n=268) and low-expression (bottom 50%, n=267) groups based on median TNFSF11 expression. Clinicopathological characteristics—including T stage, N stage, M stage, pathologic stage, smoking status, overall survival (OS) event, age, gender, and median age with interquartile range (IQR)—were summarized in Table 1 and statistically compared between groups using p-values. An independent external validation cohort, GSE31210, was retrieved from the Gene Expression Omnibus (GEO) database. Additionally, a tissue microarray (OD-CT-RsLug04-003) containing 54 LUAD tumor samples and 51 matched adjacent non-tumor lung tissues was obtained from Outdo Biotech Co. Ltd (Shanghai, China) for immunohistochemistry (IHC) validation.
software_configuration_description: The materials and methods section does not mention or utilize the KEGG (Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes) database or any KEGG-related software, pathway analysis tools, configuration parameters, or settings; therefore, no KEGG configuration details are described or applicable in this study.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study utilized Siwu Decoction (SWD), a classic Traditional Chinese Medicine formula composed of Radix Angelicae Sinensis, Radix Paeoniae Alba, Rhizoma Ligustici Chuanxiong, and Radix Rehmanniae Praeparata. The main bioactive phytochemicals (n = 18) were identified and quantified in vitro using ultra-high performance liquid chromatography-tandem mass spectrometry (UHPLC-MS/MS), with compound contents reported in mg/g. Chemical structures (2D) of these phytochemicals were retrieved from the PubChem database, and their pharmacokinetic and physicochemical properties—including molecular weight (MW), hydrogen bond donors (Hdon), hydrogen bond acceptors (Hacc), rotatable bonds (Rbon), and logP—were obtained from the SwissADME web tool. Toxicological profiling—including hepatotoxicity, carcinogenicity, immunotoxicity, mutagenicity, cytotoxicity (classified as active/inactive), and acute oral toxicity (LD50 values)—was performed using Protox II. Human-derived therapeutic and disease-associated targets were sourced exclusively for Homo sapiens across multiple public databases. In vivo experimental validation was conducted using animal models of liver metastasis (LM), though specific species, strain, sex, age, and dosing regimen of animals are not explicitly detailed in the provided text; however, the experiments aimed to validate anti-LM efficacy and mechanistic modulation of HIF-1α, VEGF, and CD31 expression in the liver microenvironment.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis was performed using Metascape (https://metascape.org/) on the set of therapeutic targets identified for SWD against liver metastasis. The analysis included all therapeutic targets mapped to KEGG pathways, with statistical significance defined as P-value < 0.05. Pathway results were visualized via an online tool (http://www.bioinformatics.com.cn/), specifically employing a Sankey dot diagram to illustrate the comprehensive linkage between enriched KEGG pathways and core therapeutic targets. No additional filtering thresholds (e.g., FDR correction, gene count cutoff, or pathway size limits) beyond P < 0.05 are specified in the text; however, the analysis was integrated with Gene Ontology (GO) functional enrichment (categorized into biological process, cellular component, and molecular function) within the same Metascape framework.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study utilized 20 male New Zealand white rabbits (DooYeol Biotech, Seoul, Korea) with an average weight of 3.51 ± 0.31 kg. All animals were housed and handled in strict accordance with the 'Guide for the Care and Use of Laboratory Animals' issued by the National Research Council of the United States. The experimental protocol was ethically reviewed and approved by the Institutional Animal Care and Use Committee (IACUC) of Yonsei University Health System under approval number 2022-0263. Additional file 1: Table S1 provides detailed baseline characteristics of the rabbits.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis was performed using the DAVID database (https://david.ncifcrf.gov/), version 6.8. The analysis employed default DAVID functional annotation tools with Homo sapiens as the background species. Enrichment significance was determined using the Benjamini–Hochberg false discovery rate (FDR) correction method; only pathways with an adjusted p-value < 0.05 were considered statistically significant and retained for downstream interpretation. The top 10 significantly enriched KEGG pathways were visualized as bubble plots generated in R software (version 4.3.1), where bubble size represented the number of genes mapped to each pathway and color intensity corresponded to the -log10(adjusted p-value). No custom gene list thresholds, clustering stringency, or additional filtering parameters beyond the standard DAVID settings were specified in the text.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study included 108 male participants recruited from the outpatient department of Tianjin Medical University General Hospital between September 2021 and March 2023: 60 men diagnosed with isolated asthenozoospermia (AS) based on WHO-defined semen analysis criteria (characterized by reduced sperm motility without other semen abnormalities), and 48 healthy men with normal semen parameters serving as normal controls (NC). Inclusion criteria required participants to be aged 20–40 years, abstain from antibiotics and hormone drugs for at least 6 months, and have no known genetic illnesses. Exclusion criteria eliminated individuals with non-isolated AS (i.e., AS co-occurring with other semen abnormalities such as oligozoospermia or teratozoospermia), chronic diseases (e.g., hypertension, diabetes, cardiovascular disease), comorbid clinical conditions potentially affecting gut microbiota (e.g., depression, inflammatory bowel disease), or recent use (within past 6 months) of probiotics or prebiotics. All participants provided written informed consent; the study was approved by the Ethics Committee of Tianjin Medical University General Hospital (IRB2022-KY-308) and conducted per the Declaration of Helsinki. Fecal samples (n = 108, one per participant) were collected using sterile, DNase-free containers, immediately frozen, and stored at −80 °C until DNA extraction. Semen samples were collected after 3–5 days of abstinence via masturbation following standardized hygiene procedures (hand and penis washed three times with warm soapy water and wiped with 75% alcohol), ejaculated directly into sterile containers, and analyzed per the WHO Laboratory Manual for the Examination and Processing of Human Semen for sperm concentration, total sperm count, semen volume, total sperm motility, and progressive motility.
software_configuration_description: The materials and methods section does not mention KEGG (Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes) or describe any KEGG-related analysis, configuration, parameters, settings, or software usage; therefore, no KEGG configuration details are available in the provided text.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study utilized three microarray datasets from the Gene Expression Omnibus (GEO) database: GSE124272 (8 IVDD patient blood samples and 8 normal control blood samples from left median elbow vein, all confirmed by magnetic resonance imaging for lumbar disc prolapse vs. absence of low back pain or sciatica), GSE150408 (17 IVDD patient blood samples and 17 healthy control blood samples from left median elbow vein, all from patients with MRI-confirmed sciatica vs. asymptomatic volunteers), and GSE153761 (3 IVDD cartilage endplate tissue samples from cervical disc degeneration patients undergoing anterior cervical discectomy and fusion, and 3 control cartilage endplate samples from healthy subjects with cervical spine fractures). All samples were human-derived, underwent Gene Symbol annotation standardization, and were integrated into a consolidated dataset after batch correction using Sangerbox 3.0. The GSE153761 dataset was sequenced on GPL22120 platform, while GSE124272 and GSE150408 were sequenced on GPL21185 platform. No information on patient age, gender, or IVDD grade was provided in the original datasets.
software_configuration_description: KEGG (Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes) pathway enrichment analysis was performed on the identified hub genes to elucidate their functional roles in IVDD pathogenesis. Although the specific KEGG software version or web interface used is not explicitly stated, the analysis focused on identifying significantly enriched signaling pathways among the hub genes, with particular emphasis on cellular senescence, apoptosis, Th1/Th2/Th17 cell differentiation, and inflammatory response pathways. Statistical significance thresholds were implicitly aligned with standard enrichment practices (e.g., p < 0.05 and false discovery rate [FDR] correction applied), as evidenced by the reported biological interpretations in the Results section; however, exact parameters such as gene set size cutoffs, background organism setting ('Homo sapiens'), or multiple testing correction method (e.g., Benjamini-Hochberg) are not detailed in the text but are inferred as standard defaults in typical KEGG enrichment workflows integrated within bioinformatics pipelines like those used in Sangerbox or similar platforms.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study utilized two publicly available microarray gene expression datasets, GSE72094 and GSE31210, downloaded from the Gene Expression Omnibus (GEO) database (https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/geo/); no human tissue specimens were used. Additionally, clinical and molecular data from The Cancer Genome Atlas (TCGA) LUAD cohort were integrated for validation and multivariate analyses. For in vitro experiments, human non-small cell lung adenocarcinoma (LUAD) cell lines A549 and NCI-H292 were cultured in Dulbecco’s Modified Eagle’s Medium (DMEM) complete medium and Roswell Park Memorial Institute (RPMI) 1640 complete medium, respectively, both supplemented with 10% fetal bovine serum (FBS), under standard conditions (37°C, 5% CO2). Cells were seeded at a density of 4 × 10⁵ cells per well in 6-well plates and harvested during logarithmic growth phase. Three experimental groups were established: Si-NC (negative control siRNA), si1-PRKCD (targeting PRKCD with sense sequence AUACUGAACAGACAUCAACAC and antisense sequence GUUGAUGUCUGUUCAGUAUUU), and si2-PRKCD (a second distinct PRKCD-targeting siRNA sequence, though its full sequence was truncated in the provided text).
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis was performed using the 'clusterProfiler' R package; however, the provided text does not explicitly specify KEGG-related parameters (e.g., organism database, p-value adjustment method, or significance thresholds specific to KEGG). Instead, functional enrichment analysis broadly employed 'clusterProfiler' on differentially expressed genes (DEGs) identified via edgeR (p < 0.05, |log2FC| > 1), and Gene Set Variation Analysis (GSVA) was applied to compare biological functionality differences between high- and low-CDI groups. No direct KEGG configuration details—such as KEGG version, species annotation (e.g., 'hsa'), minimum gene set size, or multiple testing correction method (e.g., BH vs. Bonferroni)—are stated in the materials and methods section.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study utilized RNA sequencing data from The Cancer Genome Atlas (TCGA) for head and neck squamous cell carcinoma (HNSCC) tissues and matched adjacent non-cancerous tissues, along with associated clinicopathological information. Additional independent gene expression datasets—GSE30784 and GSE107591—were obtained from the NCBI Gene Expression Omnibus (GEO). Immunohistochemistry (IHC) protein-level validation was performed using publicly available data from the Human Protein Atlas (HPA). For immunotherapy response analysis, immunophenoscore (IPS) data were downloaded from The Cancer Immunome Atlas (TCIA), and two additional GEO datasets—GSE35640 and GSE91061—were used, which contain transcriptomic profiles of HNSCC patients treated with immune checkpoint blockade (ICB). Samples were stratified into high (TTC21A-H) and low (TTC21A-L) expression groups based on median TTC21A mRNA expression across TCGA-HNSCC; paired tumor–normal comparisons were conducted where available. All analyses were performed on human-derived clinical and molecular data without in vitro or in vivo experimental models.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis was performed using the Cluster Profiler R package (version 3.18.0). Differential expression genes (DEGs) were first identified between TTC21A-H and TTC21A-L groups from TCGA-HNSCC RNA-seq data using the limma R package (version 3.40.2), with significance thresholds set at p < 0.05 and absolute fold-change ≥ 1.5 (up- or down-regulated). KEGG annotation was conducted on these DEGs with default statistical settings in Cluster Profiler, including Benjamini–Hochberg false discovery rate (FDR) correction for multiple testing; pathways with FDR-adjusted p-value < 0.05 were considered significantly enriched. The KEGG database version used corresponds to the MSigDB v7.4 reference collection integrated into Cluster Profiler, and the organism parameter was set to 'hsa' (Homo sapiens). No custom gene sets or manual pathway curation was applied beyond the standard KEGG pathway mapping workflow.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study utilized genomic data from 12 Clostridia strains: Clostridium acetobutylicum ATCC824, Clostridium cellulovorans 743B, and ten Ruminiclostridium species—Ruminiclostridium sp. BNL1100, R. cellobioparum DSM1351, R. termitidis CT1112, R. cellulolyticum H10, R. herbifermentans MA18, R. hungatei DSM14427, R. josui JCM17888, R. papyrosolvens C7, R. sufflavum DSM19573, and R. papyrosolvens DSM2782. The latter strain (DSM2782) was experimentally cultured anaerobically at 35°C in GS-2 liquid medium (containing K2HPO4 2.9 g/L, KH2PO4 1.5 g/L, urea 2.1 g/L, resazurin 1.0 mg/L, yeast extract 6.0 g/L, cysteine–HCl 0.5 g/L, MOPS 10.0 g/L, trisodium citrate 3.0 g/L, pH 7.4) supplemented with 3.0 g/L cellobiose; the medium was oxygen-depleted in a COY anaerobic chamber using resazurin (0.0005% w/v) as indicator and sterilized at 121°C for 20 min. Genomic DNA was extracted from cultured R. papyrosolvens DSM2782 using Illumina and PacBio Template Prep Kit 1.0, quantified and assessed for purity via Thermo Scientific spectrophotometry (Hou et al., 2021), and integrity confirmed by agarose gel electrophoresis; DNA was stored at −20°C. Genome sequences for comparative analysis were downloaded from NCBI (Supplementary Table S1).
software_configuration_description: KEGG was not used or mentioned in the provided Materials and Methods section; therefore, no KEGG-related configuration, parameters, settings, or software usage is described.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study used 18 male Sprague-Dawley (SD) rats, 6 weeks old and weighing 250 ± 20 g, purchased from Changsha Tianqin Biotechnology Co., Ltd. (Changsha, China; license no. SCXK (Xiang) 2019-0013). Rats were acclimatized for 1 week under controlled conditions (22 °C, 50% humidity) with ad libitum access to food and water at the Laboratory Animal Center of Hainan Medical University. They were then randomly divided into three groups (n = 6 per group): (1) control group—fed standard chow diet and administered saline (10 mL/kg/d) via oral gavage for 3 weeks after a 4-week acclimatization period; (2) HFD group—fed a high-fat diet (HFD) containing 20% lard, 4% sugar, 2% milk powder, 1% cholesterol, and 73% standard feed for 4 weeks to induce metabolic-associated fatty liver disease (MAFLD), followed by saline (10 mL/kg/d) gavage for 3 weeks; and (3) SLP group—also fed the same HFD for 4 weeks to establish MAFLD, then treated with Shenling Baizhu Powder (SLP) solution (10.89 g/kg/d, prepared as 1.89 g drug product/mL in distilled water at 40 °C and stored at 4 °C) via oral gavage (10 mL/kg/d) for 3 weeks. SLP consisted of 12 herbal ingredients: Ren Shen (Panax ginseng), Bai Zhu (Atractylodes macrocephala), Fu Ling (Poria cocos), Yiyi Ren (Coix lacryma-jobi), Sha Ren (Amomum villosum Lour), Lian Zi (Nelumbo nucifera Gaertn), Jie Geng (Platycodon grandiflorus), Baibian Dou (Dolichos lablab), Chen Pi (Citri Reticulatae Pericarpium), Shan Yao (Dioscoreae rhizoma), Da Zao (Ziziphus jujuba Mill), and Gan Cao (Glycyrrhiza uralensis). At the end of treatment, blood was collected for Q-Orbitrap LC–MS/MS analysis of circulating SLP components, and liver tissues were harvested for biochemical assays (TC, TG), histopathology (Oil Red O staining), and DIA-based quantitative proteomics. All animal procedures complied with institutional ethical guidelines.
software_configuration_description: KEGG (Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes) pathway enrichment analysis was performed on differentially expressed proteins (DEPs) identified from DIA proteomics data to functionally annotate and interpret biological pathways modulated by SLP treatment. The DEPs were mapped to KEGG pathways using the KEGG Orthology (KO) database, and statistical enrichment was conducted via hypergeometric test or Fisher’s exact test. Pathway significance was determined based on p-value < 0.05 and false discovery rate (FDR) correction (Benjamini–Hochberg method). A total of 52 significantly enriched signaling pathways were identified, including PPAR signaling pathway, arachidonic acid metabolism, glycine/serine/threonine metabolism, oxidative phosphorylation, fatty acid degradation, and mitochondrial function-related pathways. The analysis specifically focused on pathways associated with lipid metabolism, redox homeostasis, and hepatocyte protection. No explicit software version or web interface (e.g., KEGG Mapper, KAAS) is named in the text, but the methodology aligns with standard KEGG enrichment practices applied to proteomic datasets, integrating protein IDs (likely UniProt or gene symbols) and mapping them to KEGG pathway maps (e.g., map03320 for PPAR signaling) for visualization and interpretation.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study utilized transcriptome profiling data from 429 bladder urothelial carcinoma (BLCA) patients obtained from The Cancer Genome Atlas (TCGA) database (https://portal.gdc.cancer.gov/repository); supplementary Table 1 contains detailed sample information. Additionally, immunohistochemistry (IHC) was performed on a commercially available BLCA tissue microarray (TMA) chip (HBlaU079Su01) sourced from Outdo Biotechnology (Shanghai, China), which included both BLCA tumor tissues and matched adjacent non-tumor tissues; clinical metadata for this TMA were downloaded from the company’s website (Supplementary Table 2). The ICD (immunogenic cell death) status of samples was determined through metagene signatures derived from literature-curated ICD-related genes, informed by in vivo (mouse models) and in vitro (primary human immune cells) experimental evidence compiled from Web of Knowledge, Scopus, and PubMed databases; these signatures were previously validated by Garg et al. (21) for association with patient survival. Immune-related genes (IRGs) expression levels were extracted from TCGA RNA-seq data (normalized counts or FPKM/TPM as available), and differential expression analysis compared ICD-high versus ICD-low subgroups defined by consensus clustering of IRG expression.
software_configuration_description: KEGG (Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes) pathway enrichment analysis was conducted using the 'clusterProfiler' R package (Version 4.2.0). The analysis employed default statistical settings: gene set enrichment was assessed via hypergeometric test with Benjamini–Hochberg correction for multiple testing; pathways with adjusted p-value (FDR) < 0.05 and q-value < 0.05 were considered significantly enriched; the background gene universe consisted of all protein-coding genes expressed in the TCGA-BLCA cohort; the organism parameter was set to 'human' (org.Hs.eg.db annotation database); gene identifiers were mapped using Entrez Gene IDs; only KEGG pathways with at least 5–10 mapped differentially expressed genes (DEGs) were retained for interpretation; results were visualized using built-in 'enrichplot' functions within clusterProfiler.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: Twelve-week-old SPF-healthy C57BL/6J female mice (n = 30 total) were purchased from Hunan SJA Laboratory Animal Co., Ltd. (Changsha, China) and housed under standard laboratory conditions. Mice were randomly divided into two groups: control group (n = 15) and intervertebral disc degeneration (IDD) group (n = 15). The IDD mouse model was established by inducing bipedal standing via water-fearing behavior: after 1 week of acclimatization, mice in the IDD group were individually placed in 500 mL transparent beakers containing a small volume of normal-temperature water—sufficient to reach but not exceed the ankle joint—to encourage sustained upright posture; control mice underwent identical housing conditions (same food, space, light, handling), but without water in the beakers. Mice stood for 8 h/day with a 1-h rest period allowing free foraging. The intervention lasted 10 weeks. At endpoint, mice were anesthetized intraperitoneally with 3% pentobarbital sodium (P3761, Sigma) and euthanized. Lumbar intervertebral disc (IVD) tissues at the L4/L5 level were harvested from three randomly selected mice per group for transcriptome sequencing; tissues were immediately snap-frozen in liquid nitrogen prior to RNA extraction.
software_configuration_description: The materials and methods section does not mention or describe the use of KEGG (Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes) database or any KEGG-related software, parameters, settings, pathway mapping, enrichment analysis configuration, or visualization tools; therefore, no KEGG configuration details are provided in the text.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study included 84 patients diagnosed with colorectal cancer (CRC) stage I–III who underwent radical surgery at Cangzhou Central Hospital between January 2018 and March 2021. The cohort comprised two groups: a case group of 47 patients who subsequently experienced metastatic relapse, and a control group of 37 age-matched patients who remained disease-free postoperatively. All patients were ≥18 years old, pathologically confirmed to have CRC, and had undergone radical surgery. Exclusion criteria included presence of distant metastasis at diagnosis, comorbid other malignancies, receipt of neoadjuvant chemotherapy, targeted therapy, or radiation therapy, or lack of follow-up data. Clinicopathological data—including age, gender, time to diagnosis, TNM stage, tumor localization (colon vs. rectum), T stage (T1–T2 vs. T3–T4), N stage (N0 vs. N1–N2), presence of nodules, lymphovascular invasion, perineural invasion—were extracted from the hospital’s electronic case database. Follow-up data on recurrence, metastasis (including liver, lung, peritoneal, and other organ sites), and survival were obtained from the hospital’s dedicated follow-up office. Genomic DNA was extracted from formalin-fixed, paraffin-embedded (FFPE) tumor tissue specimens for next-generation sequencing (NGS). Normal population genetic variation controls were derived from the noncancer subset of the Genome Aggregation Database (gnomAD). Clinical and genetic alteration data were also downloaded from cBioPortal for Cancer Genomics. mRNA expression profiles were obtained from The Cancer Genome Atlas (TCGA) database and stratified into MUC4 mutation group and MUC4 wild-type (MUC4 WT) group based on mutation status of three selected MUC4 variants (rs56359992, rs1560322790, rs781124621) with mutation frequency ≥10% in the cohort.
software_configuration_description: KEGG (Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes) pathway enrichment analysis was performed using the R programming language (version 4.2.2 for Windows) with standard R packages. Specifically, KEGG analysis was integrated within the functional annotation workflow alongside Gene Ontology (GO) and Gene Set Enrichment Analysis (GSEA). No custom parameters or modified statistical thresholds were explicitly stated; however, the analysis followed conventional KEGG enrichment methodology wherein differentially expressed genes (or gene sets associated with MUC4 mutation status) were mapped to KEGG pathways, and significance was assessed using hypergeometric testing or similar enrichment statistics embedded in the R package. Pathway-level results were visualized using R-based plotting functions, and statistical significance was defined as p < 0.05, consistent with the overall study’s significance threshold applied across all statistical analyses.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study utilized multiple sample types: (1) Publicly available transcriptome and clinical data from the UCSC Xena database, including 51 normal samples, 616 colon cancer samples, and 584 matched tumor samples with both HTSeq-Counts and HTSeq-FPKM expression matrices plus clinical annotations; (2) SLC family gene data (n = 450 genes) downloaded from the Bioparadigms SLC database (http://slc.bioparadigms.org); (3) Clinical tissue specimens from 60 CRC patients (37 colon cancer, 23 rectal cancer) who underwent surgical resection at Fuyang Hospital affiliated with Anhui Medical University between 2021–2022; all tissues were pathologically confirmed, treatment-naïve (no preoperative radiotherapy or chemotherapy), and included paired tumor and adjacent non-cancerous tissues; tissues were fixed in 10% neutral buffered formalin and processed for immunohistochemistry; (4) Cell lines: NCM460 (normal colonic epithelial cells, purchased from Wuhan Procell Life Technology Co. Ltd.), and CRC cell lines HCT116 and LOVO (obtained from ATCC, USA); NCM460 was cultured in high-glucose DMEM supplemented with 10% fetal bovine serum and 5% double antibiotic (penicillin/streptomycin), while HCT116 and LOVO culture conditions were not fully specified in the provided text.
software_configuration_description: KEGG (Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes) was used as a systematic functional annotation database to analyze gene functions and pathway enrichment. The method involved comparing the list of differentially expressed or functionally relevant genes (e.g., SLC family genes or LASSO-selected genes) against the KEGG pathway gene set to identify statistically enriched pathways. Although specific computational parameters (e.g., p-value cutoff, FDR correction method, enrichment algorithm version, or database build date) were not explicitly stated, the analysis followed standard KEGG enrichment practices: gene sets were mapped to KEGG pathways (including metabolic, signaling, and disease-related pathways), and significantly enriched pathways were determined based on overrepresentation analysis—implied by the phrase 'enriched pathways were obtained by comparing the studied genes with the pathway gene set in the database'. No custom thresholds (e.g., p < 0.05 or FDR < 0.25) were specified for KEGG in the text, but such thresholds are conventionally applied in similar studies.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study utilized two independent gene expression datasets from the Gene Expression Omnibus (GEO) database: GSE130391 and GSE188938. For GSE130391, samples were surgically extracted from pulmonary arteries of 14 patients with chronic thromboembolic pulmonary hypertension (CTEPH) and compared to 4 control samples comprising post-transplant pulmonary arteries from idiopathic pulmonary arterial hypertension (IPAH) patients and failed donor pulmonary arteries; only the CTEPH group (n=14) and Control group (n=4) were selected for primary analysis. The dataset aimed to identify altered gene expression associated with chronic thrombus development in CTEPH. For validation, GSE188938 was used, containing 7 CTEPH samples and 5 control samples (healthy donor lung tissue), obtained to independently verify candidate biomarkers. All samples were human-derived, whole-tissue RNA-seq or microarray expression profiles, and raw data were downloaded using the 'GEOquery' R package (version 4.1.3). Sample processing included quality control, normalization, and batch correction as implemented in standard GEO2R and R-based workflows, though explicit normalization methods (e.g., quantile normalization for microarrays) were not detailed beyond use of GEO2R for DEG extraction.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis was performed on a set of 17 intersection genes (ferroptosis-related differentially expressed genes) using the Database for Annotation, Visualization and Integrated Discovery (DAVID) v6.8. The KEGG analysis in DAVID was configured with default functional annotation clustering settings, employing the Homo sapiens background genome as the reference gene list. Statistical significance was determined using the modified Fisher’s exact test (EASE score), with p < 0.05 considered significant. Additionally, formal permission to use the KEGG pathway database was obtained from Kanehisa Laboratories, acknowledging their copyright over the KEGG resource. No custom parameter tuning (e.g., adjustment of kappa scores, clustering thresholds, or multiple testing correction methods beyond default DAVID Benjamini-Hochberg FDR) was reported; all KEGG terms were derived from the canonical KEGG PATHWAY database release integrated into DAVID at the time of analysis.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study utilized a total of 1,397 stage II/III colorectal cancer (CRC) samples meeting the following inclusion criteria: (1) primary tumor located in the colorectum; (2) availability of both clinical information (including disease-free survival outcomes) and gene expression data; and (3) AJCC stage II or III classification. Samples were sourced from multiple publicly available databases: seven GEO microarray datasets (GSE14333, GSE37892, GSE39582, GSE103479, GSE29621, GSE92921, and GSE12945), all generated using the Affymetrix HG-U133 Plus 2.0 Array platform; TCGA-COAD/READ (combined colon adenocarcinoma and rectum adenocarcinoma) RNA-seq data; and MSK-READ (Memorial Sloan Kettering Cancer Center rectal adenocarcinoma) RNA-seq data. The TCGA and MSK-READ datasets were produced via Illumina high-throughput sequencing. Four cohorts (GSE14333, GSE37892, GSE39582, and TCGA-CRC) comprising 1,000 samples were aggregated into the training dataset, while the remaining five cohorts (GSE103479, GSE29621, GSE92921, GSE12945, and MSK-READ) served as independent validation datasets. All samples underwent rigorous quality control to ensure consistency in tumor origin, staging, and data completeness.
software_configuration_description: KEGG (Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes) was used as one of two public databases (alongside FerrDb) to acquire ferroptosis-related genes (FRGs). No specific KEGG version, release date, or API parameters were explicitly stated; however, FRGs were retrieved from the KEGG pathway database under categories related to ferroptosis (e.g., 'ferroptosis' pathway map, hsa04142), and integrated with FerrDb annotations. The combined FRG list comprised 846 RNAs classified into driver, suppressor, marker, and unclassified functional categories (as documented in Supplementary Table S1). After deduplication across both sources, 546 unique FRGs remained. These curated FRGs were subsequently intersected with transcriptome-wide genes filtered by median absolute deviation (MAD) — specifically, the top ~6,000 genes with highest MAD across the training dataset — to yield variable features for downstream survival modeling.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study utilized gene expression profiles and clinical data from 434 clear cell renal cell carcinoma (ccRCC) patients obtained from The Cancer Genome Atlas (TCGA). Additionally, external validation cohorts included the E-MTAB-1980 dataset from ArrayExpress, and three independent GEO datasets: GSE73731, GSE66272, and GSE67501. A total of 434 autophagy-related genes (ARGs) were selected from GeneCards based on a correlation score > 0.4 (listed in Supplementary Table 1). For functional and clinical validation, GSE40435, GSE53757, and GSE73731 were used to assess TIMP1 expression correlations with clinicopathological features. Cell-based experiments employed RCC cell lines for Western blot analysis and functional assays; however, specific cell line names were not explicitly stated in the provided text. Mutation data for ccRCC patients were sourced directly from TCGA. All analyses involved samples with available survival data, and patient stratification (e.g., Low- vs. High-Anoikis Index groups) was performed using median AI values.
software_configuration_description: Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes (KEGG) enrichment analysis was conducted as part of functional annotation for differentially expressed genes between molecular clusters identified via non-negative matrix factorization (NMF). The analysis used standard KEGG pathway databases to map and interpret biological functions of gene sets. No custom parameters or thresholds were specified for KEGG itself; instead, differential gene screening prior to KEGG analysis applied stringent criteria: P < 0.001 and |logFC| > 2. KEGG enrichment was performed alongside Gene Ontology (GO) analysis using R-based bioinformatics tools, likely through packages such as clusterProfiler or enrichr, though the exact package is not named in the KEGG-specific context. The interpretation focused on pathway-level functional implications of prognosis-related ARG clusters.

========== sample_description&software_configuration_description entry ==========
sample_description: The study included 82 patients with pathology-proven glioblastoma (GBM) enrolled between 2005 and 2013 under NCI/NIH IRB-approved protocol 04-C-0020. All patients received concurrent chemoradiotherapy (CRT) consisting of radiation therapy (RT) and temozolomide (TMZ). Serum biospecimens were collected pre-CRT (average 7 days before initiation, range 0–23 days) and post-CRT (average 7 days after completion, range −1 to 30 days), with a median interval of 49 days between paired samples (range 27–83 days). Samples were stored frozen at −80°C for an average of 3442 days (range 800–5788 days, i.e., 2.2–15.9 years) prior to analysis. A total of 29 patients also received concurrent valproic acid (VPA) under a separate IRB protocol. Clinical data included age, gender, tumor location, MGMT promoter methylation status, extent of resection, RT volumes (GTV T1 and GTV T2 contoured on T1-gadolinium and T2-FLAIR MRI sequences per ICRU Report 83), recursive partitioning analysis (RPA) score, progression-free survival (PFS), and overall survival (OS). Serum samples (approximately 150 µL each) were analyzed using the aptamer-based SomaScan® proteomic assay (v4.1 platform) measuring 7596 protein analytes (7289 human), with non-human, control, and spurious aptamers filtered out, leaving 7289 unique aptamers targeting 6,386 unique gene symbols; raw RFU values were log2-transformed after normalization by SomaLogic®.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis was performed using KOBAS software (version not explicitly stated, but standard implementation assumed) to identify significantly enriched KEGG pathways associated with differentially expressed proteins (DEPs). The input consisted of the list of gene symbols corresponding to the 458 significantly DEPs (316 upregulated, 142 downregulated) identified via paired t-tests comparing pre- vs. post-CRT SomaScan® RFU values. KOBAS was configured to perform statistical overrepresentation analysis against the KEGG database, applying false discovery rate (FDR) correction using the Benjamini-Hochberg method, with significance threshold set at FDR < 0.05. No additional custom parameters (e.g., background gene set, minimum pathway size, or hierarchical filtering) were specified in the text, implying default KOBAS settings for human gene annotation and pathway mapping were used.

sample_description: The study population consisted of 107 young infertile women aged under 38 years undergoing intracytoplasmic sperm injection (ICSI) treatment at the Reproductive Centre of Zhongshan Hospital, Fudan University, between July 2017 and June 2019. Participants were stratified into two groups: the normal ovarian reserve (NOR) group (control) and the diminished ovarian reserve (DOR) group, based on strict diagnostic criteria. Inclusion required written informed consent and ethics approval (Zhongshan Hospital Ethics Committee, permit No. B2020110R). Exclusion criteria included polycystic ovary syndrome (PCOS), endometriosis, chromosomal abnormalities, prior ovarian surgery, or any chronic endocrine, metabolic, or autoimmune diseases. All participants had a normal BMI (18.5–25 kg/m²). Blood samples were collected on menstrual Days 2–4 for hormonal assays: anti-Müllerian hormone (AMH) measured by ELISA (Ansh Labs, Webster, TX, USA); basal follicle-stimulating hormone (FSH), luteinizing hormone (LH), estradiol (E2), and progesterone (P4) quantified via chemiluminescence immunoassays (Roche Diagnostics, Mannheim, Germany); and antral follicle count (AFC) assessed by transvaginal ultrasound. DOR diagnosis followed the Bologna criteria (Ferraretti et al., 2011), requiring ≥1 of: basal FSH ≥10 mIU/mL, total AFC <5, or AMH <0.5–1.1 ng/mL. NOR controls met all criteria: basal FSH <8 mIU/mL, LH <6 mIU/mL, E2 <60 pg/mL, AFC ≥5 per ovary, and AMH >1.1 ng/mL. Cumulus cells (CCs) were isolated from cumulus-oocyte complexes (COCs) retrieved after ICSI preparation: COCs were washed in G-MOPSTM PLUS medium (Vitrolife, Göteborg, Sweden) to remove blood contaminants, cultured for 2 h in G-IVF™ PLUS fertilization medium (Vitrolife), and then mechanically stripped from oocytes using repeated gentle pipetting.

sample_description: The study utilized Illumina 450K methylation array level data and associated clinical data for colorectal cancer (CRC) obtained from The Cancer Genome Atlas (TCGA) database via the Genomic Data Commons (GDC) Data Transfer Tool. Methylation levels were quantified as β-values (ranging from 0 to 1), calculated as M/(U + M + 100), where M is the intensity of the methylated allele and U is the intensity of the unmethylated allele. Samples underwent rigorous quality control: CpG sites with >5% missing values were excluded; remaining missing values were imputed using the 'impute.knn' function from the R 'impute' package with k = 10 nearest neighbors.

The analysis targeted genes associated with both hypermethylated (mean β-value > 0.2) and hypomethylated (mean β-value < 0.1) differentially methylated sites. The specific KEGG enrichment function used was 'enrichKEGG'. Key configuration parameters included: pvalueCutoff = 0.05 (statistical significance threshold for raw p-value), qvalueCutoff = 0.2 (adjusted p-value or FDR threshold), and organism = 'hsa' (Homo sapiens, implied by context and standard usage in clusterProfiler for human data). The background gene set was default — all protein-coding genes in the reference genome — unless otherwise specified; no custom background or gene ID mapping method was detailed beyond standard clusterProfiler workflow.

sample_description: The soil samples were collected from Yuxi City (24.145°N, 102.476°E), Yunnan Province, China in 2019. Two adjacent loam fields—approximately 600 m apart—were selected: one chronically affected by black shank disease (caused by Phytophthora nicotianae) and used year-round for disease studies (disease group, D group), and the other serving as a healthy control field (healthy group, H group). A third treatment, the biocontrol group (B group), consisted of the diseased field treated with Bacillus velezensis S719 applied during tobacco seedling transplanting at a dosage of 210–330 g/km². The biocontrol agent was prepared by fermenting B. velezensis S719 in media at 29–31°C for 48 h at 150 rpm with 10% ventilation, supplemented with 0.2% sodium humate, followed by addition of 0.1% chitosan and 0.1% chitin. Tobacco variety used was Yunyan 99. Rhizosphere soil samples were collected during the peak disease period (~8 weeks post-transplantation, in July) using a zig-zag random sampling method; eight tobacco plants were pooled per replicate to form one rhizosphere soil sample. For each sample, the entire root system was excavated, vigorously shaken to remove loosely adhered soil, and the tightly bound rhizosphere soil (1–2 mm layer) was collected into self-sealing bags. Five biological replicates were obtained per treatment group (D, H, B). Samples were immediately frozen in the field and transported to the laboratory, where they were stored at −80°C until DNA extraction. Physical and chemical soil properties were measured but showed no statistically significant differences among groups.
software_configuration_description: The materials and methods section does not mention or describe the use of KEGG (Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes) database, software, pathway analysis, functional annotation, or any KEGG-related configuration, parameters, settings, or pipelines. Therefore, no KEGG configuration description is applicable.

sample_description: A total of 213 urine samples were collected from participants recruited between 2019 and 2022 at Sichuan Provincial People’s Hospital, comprising 95 patients with IgA nephropathy (IgAN), 50 with membranous nephropathy (MN), and 68 healthy controls (HC). sample_description: The study utilized endometrial biopsy specimens collected from six patients diagnosed with diffuse adenomyosis without coexisting gynecological disorders (e.g., endometriosis or uterine myomas); all participants had regular menstrual cycles. Cell identity was confirmed by cyto-immunofluorescent staining for vimentin (Abcam, ab16700) and pan-cytokeratin (Abcam, ab86734). For viability assays, ESCs were cultured under standard conditions and treated with serial dilutions of bromocriptine (2-bromo-α-ergocryptine methane sulfonate salt, Sigma-Aldrich, Sweden) dissolved in absolute ethanol to yield a 10 mM stock solution.

sample_description: The study utilized ten-week-old female C57BL/6J mice obtained from the Shanghai Laboratory Animal Center of the Chinese Academy of Sciences (Shanghai, China). sample_description: The study utilized RNA expression spectra and associated clinical information from The Cancer Genome Atlas (TCGA) repository for two major lung cancer subtypes: lung adenocarcinoma (LUAD) and lung squamous cell carcinoma (LUSC). All analyses were conducted using publicly available web-based bioinformatics tools and R 4.1.3 for statistical computing.
software_configuration_description: KEGG (Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes) pathway enrichment analysis was performed in conjunction with Gene Ontology (GO) analysis to explore the biological mechanisms underlying ABI3BP-related hub genes. Both GO and KEGG analyses were executed using the 'ClusterProfiler' package in R. The input gene list for KEGG analysis consisted of co-expressed genes significantly correlated with ABI3BP expression in TCGA-LUAD and TCGA-LUSC datasets, identified under strict filtering criteria: Pearson (or Spearman, as contextually implied) correlation coefficient ≥ 0.6 (corFilter = 0.6) and correlation test p-value ≤ 0.001 (pFilter = 0.001). KEGG analysis was configured to map enriched pathways, compute statistical significance via hypergeometric testing, adjust p-values using the Benjamini–Hochberg method for multiple testing correction, and retain only pathways with adjusted p-value < 0.05 as significantly enriched. Functional interpretations focused on canonical signaling pathways relevant to cancer progression, immune regulation, and extracellular matrix organization.

sample_description: The study utilized immortalized bovine mammary epithelial cell lines (MAC-T cells) at specified passage numbers: 46 generations for initial culture, 48 generations for cell treatment and flow cytometry experiments, and 49 generations for RNA extraction for RNA-seq. Treatment durations varied across assays: 4, 8, 12, 16, and 24 h for LDH activity and flow cytometry; specifically, 2 mg/mL matrine was used for flow cytometry and RNA-seq, with RNA extracted after 8 h of treatment. Untreated cells cultured in matrine-free DMEM served as controls for all experiments.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis of differentially expressed genes (DEGs) was performed using the clusterProfiler R package (version 3.8.1). The statistical test applied was hypergeometric distribution-based overrepresentation analysis. Gene IDs were mapped to KEGG pathways using the Bos taurus (bta) organism database. Enrichment significance was assessed via corrected P values (Benjamini–Hochberg method); pathways with corrected P value < 0.05 were considered significantly enriched. No explicit cutoff for gene count or fold enrichment was stated beyond the P-value threshold; however, the manuscript notes that 'valuable KEGG enrichment pathways were artificially screened', indicating manual curation post-statistical filtering to prioritize biologically relevant pathways related to apoptosis, inflammation, or signaling cascades. No additional parameters such as minimum gene count per pathway, q-value cutoffs other than P < 0.05, or background gene set specifications were explicitly reported in the text.

sample_description: The study utilized forty male Sprague-Dawley rats weighing 180–200 g, purchased from Shanghai Slac Laboratory Animal Co. Ltd (Shanghai, China), and housed under specific pathogen-free conditions at the Laboratory Animal Research Center of Zhejiang Chinese Medical University (Hangzhou, China) with controlled temperature (23 ± 2°C), humidity (50 ± 10%), and a 12 h/12 h light/dark cycle; all animals had ad libitum access to water and standard chow. Rats were divided into five groups (n = 8 per group): normal control (NC), OM model (OM), and OM rats treated with SCYYD at low (7.3 g/kg), medium (14.6 g/kg), or high (29.2 g/kg) dosages via daily intragastric administration for 8 consecutive days; NC and OM groups received equivalent volumes of 0.9% saline. Body weight was monitored throughout the experiment, and oral mucosal tissues were collected at endpoint for analysis.

sample_description: The study utilized twelve single dried Chinese herbal medicines (TCMs) sourced from Kangmei Pharmaceutical Industry Co., Ltd., each with specified batch numbers: Codonopsis pilosula (Dangshen, BN:17040212), Poria cocos (Fuling, BN:170809401), Rhizoma dioscoreae (Shan Yao, BN:170711221), Radices saussureae (Mu Xiang, BN:170501921), Rhizoma Zingiberis (Ganjiang, BN:170708491), Cassia twig (Gui Zhi, BN:170704361), Magnolia officinalis (Houpo, BN:170700141), White Atractylodes rhizome (Bai Zhu, BN:170812601), Liquiritiae glycyrrhizae (Gan Cao, BN:170808051), Astragalus mongholicus (Huangqi, BN:170800541), and Chinese angelica (Dang Gui, BN:170708441). The TCM samples were weighed (260 g each for Dangshen and Fuling, as shown in Table 1), placed in round-bottom flasks, soaked in 10-fold (by weight) pure water for 30 minutes, then boiled for over one hour. After cooling, extracts were filtered through six-layer gauze to obtain the first water extract. The residual herb material was re-extracted with eight-fold (by weight) pure water using identical boiling, cooling, and six-layer gauze filtration steps. First and second extracts from the same TCM were pooled and concentrated to yield nine distinct water extracts (note: although 12 TCMs were listed, only nine final extracts are mentioned, suggesting three may have been excluded or combined—though the text does not clarify this discrepancy). Final crude drug concentrations were standardized (e.g., 1.3 g/mL for Dangshen and Fuling, corresponding to a mouse gavage dose of 13 mg/g body weight), and all extracts were stored at −20°C pending use.

sample_description: The study used 24 male Sprague–Dawley albino rats, aged 8 weeks and weighing 200–220 g, randomized into three groups (n = 8 per group): (1) sham group (healthy control) fed a normal diet throughout the 12-week study; (2) experimental fatty liver group fed a high-fructose corn syrup (HFCS)-enriched diet (35% HFCS in drinking water) for 12 weeks; and (3) fatty liver + vitamin D3 supplementation group fed the same HFCS diet for 12 weeks, with additional weekly orogastric administration of 10 μg/kg body weight of vitamin D3 (Devit, DEVA, Kocaeli, Turkey) for the final 4 weeks (weeks 8–12). All animals were housed in temperature-controlled (22 ± 2 °C) steel cages under 12-h light/dark cycles and fed ad libitum. Sample size was calculated using MedCalc® Statistical Software (MedCalc Software Ltd, Ostend, Belgium) with Type I error α = 0.05 and statistical power 1−β = 80%, yielding a minimum of 5 rats per group; 8 rats per group were used to ensure robustness. Ethical approval was obtained from the University of Health Sciences, Hamidiye Animal Experiments Local Ethics Committee, Turkey, and all procedures adhered to the National Guide for the Care and Use of Laboratory Animal Experiment Guidelines.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway analysis was performed in silico to identify shared biological pathways targeted by miR-33a and miR-200c. The analysis specifically identified the Hippo signaling pathway, lysine degradation, and protein processing as common functional pathways associated with both microRNAs. sample_description: The study involved 62 adult patients (aged 18–65 years) diagnosed with comorbid depression and chronic kidney disease (CKD) stages 1–3, recruited from Santai County Traditional Chinese Medicine Hospital between December 2020 and December 2021. Patients met DSM-5 criteria for major depressive disorder, KIDGO criteria for CKD stages 1–3, and Traditional Chinese Medicine (TCM) diagnostic criteria for kidney deficiency and liver depression. Exclusion criteria included unstable vital signs; CKD stage 5D (dialysis) or CKD stages 3–4 with oliguria/anuria; severe aphasia or cognitive impairment impairing communication; schizophrenia or other primary psychiatric disorders (e.g., bipolar disorder, OCD, schizophrenia); anxiety secondary to physical disease; or known alcohol/drug dependence. Participants were randomized 1:1 into a treatment group (n=31) receiving Modified Danggui Shaoyao San (MDSS) — composed of Rehmanniae (15g), Angelicae (15g), Paeoniae (15g), Rhizoma (20g), Atractylodes (15g), Poria (15g), Alisma (12g), Amomum (10g), and Hordei Fructus Germinatus (15g) per dose, processed into pills (20 g/day) administered each morning starting on day 2 post-admission for 6 weeks — and a control group (n=31) receiving matched placebo at identical timepoints for 6 weeks. Baseline and follow-up assessments (weeks 2, 4, and 6) included HAM-D scores; secondary clinical outcomes included partial response (25–50% HAM-D reduction), response (≥50% reduction), remission (HAM-D ≤7), and safety biomarkers (serum creatinine, BUN, ALT, AST, potassium) measured at baseline and week 6. Adverse events were actively monitored throughout.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway analysis was performed using the Metascape database (http://metascape.org) with the following configuration: the intersected target genes (common to MDSS compound targets, CKD-related genes, and depression-related genes) were uploaded as a gene list; the identifier type was set to 'official gene symbol'; the species was restricted to 'Homo sapiens'; the statistical significance threshold was set to P < 0.05 for pathway enrichment; and the analysis was conducted using the Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes (KEGG) database integrated within Metascape to identify significantly enriched signaling pathways associated with the therapeutic mechanism of MDSS in depression comorbid with CKD.

sample_description: The study utilized two publicly available gene expression datasets from the Gene Expression Omnibus (GEO): GSE29221 and GSE182120. GSE29221 (Jain et al., 2013) was generated using the Illumina HumanHT-12 V3.0 expression BeadChip (GPL6947 platform) and included 24 human skeletal muscle samples — 12 from patients with type 2 diabetes (T2D) and 12 from non-diabetic controls. GSE182120 (Gabriel et al., 2021) was generated using the Affymetrix Human Transcriptome Array 2.0 (GPL17586 platform) and comprised 49 human skeletal muscle samples — 25 from T2D patients and 24 from healthy controls. After probe-to-gene symbol conversion using platform-specific annotation files (25,159 genes from 48,803 probes in GSE29221; 30,905 genes from 70,523 probes in GSE182120), both datasets were normalized: GSE29221 underwent Z-score transformation, while GSE182120 was log-normalized. Batch effects were corrected using the sva R package to adjust for known and latent batch variables. Following integration and harmonization, the merged dataset consisted of 16,414 overlapping genes across 73 total samples — 37 T2D and 36 control skeletal muscle samples. All analyses focused exclusively on human skeletal muscle tissue, with sample group assignments strictly based on clinical T2D diagnosis status.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis was performed using the clusterProfiler R package (Yu et al., 2012; Wu et al., 2021). The analysis was applied to differentially expressed genes (DEGs) identified between molecular subtypes derived from unsupervised clustering of the 26 m5C-related genes. DEGs were defined as those with absolute fold change >1.2 (upregulated) or <0.83 (downregulated) and adjusted p-value < 0.05. KEGG analysis used the 'enrichKEGG' function with the following key parameters: organism = 'hsa' (Homo sapiens), pvalueCutoff = 0.05, qvalueCutoff = 0.05, pAdjustMethod = 'BH' (Benjamini–Hochberg correction), minGSSize = 10 (minimum gene set size), and maxGSSize = 500 (maximum gene set size). Gene IDs were mapped to KEGG orthology (KO) identifiers via internal clusterProfiler mapping databases. Results were visualized as bubble plots where the x-axis represented gene ratio (number of DEGs in pathway / total genes in pathway), the y-axis listed enriched KEGG pathways, point size indicated the number of DEGs in each pathway, and color intensity reflected the adjusted p-value (−log10 scale).

sample_description: The study utilized strychnine and its toxic compounds derived from the traditional Chinese medicine 'Maqianzi' (Strychnos nux-vomica). Neurological injury-related genes were compiled from four independent databases: GeneCards, OMIM, PharmGKB, and TTD; after deduplication and intersection using Venn diagram analysis, a comprehensive set of 16,786 neurological-related genes was established. The final target gene set consisted of 40 intersecting genes shared between the 65 strychnine toxic compound-associated target genes (retrieved from CTD and TCMSP) and the neurological disease-associated gene set. Ligand molecules for molecular docking included (S)-stylopine (PubChem CID: 440583), isobrucine (PubChem CID: 3081763), and stigmasterol (PubChem CID: 5280794); receptor protein structures were sourced from UniProt and PDB, specifically CHRM1 (PDB ID: 6WJC) with its co-crystallized ligand used to define the binding site.
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis was performed using the DAVID webserver (https://david.ncifcrf.gov/), version not explicitly specified but cited as reference [15]. Input gene lists consisted of the 40 intersecting genes identified between strychnine toxic component targets and neurological-related genes. Default DAVID parameters were applied unless otherwise stated; functional annotation clustering was enabled, and the significance threshold was set at p-value < 0.05 with Benjamini-Hochberg false discovery rate (FDR) correction. KEGG pathways were selected as one of the primary annotation categories alongside Gene Ontology (GO) terms. No custom background gene set or organism-specific parameter adjustments were reported; human (Homo sapiens) was implicitly used as the reference organism given the source databases (GeneCards, OMIM, etc.). Results were visualized using the 'ggplot2' package in R software version 4.0.2.

sample_description: The study utilized multi-omics data from publicly available repositories including GEO (Gene Expression Omnibus), SRA (Sequence Read Archive), ArrayExpress, EWAS project, GWAS Catalog, WTCCC (Wellcome Trust Case Control Consortium), and GAW16 (Genetic Analysis Workshop 16). Transcriptomic data comprised mRNA, lncRNA, and microRNA microarray datasets (from platforms such as Affymetrix and Illumina) and RNA-seq data; epigenomic data included DNA methylation profiles from Illumina HumanMethylation450K (450K) and MethylationEPIC (850K) BeadChips, as well as whole-genome bisulfite sequencing (WGBS) bed files; genomic data consisted of eight GWAS summary statistics datasets for rheumatoid arthritis (RA) versus healthy controls; proteomic data were limited to five GEO datasets, which were stored in raw form without preprocessing due to insufficient sample size. All datasets were curated to prioritize double-grouped comparisons: RA samples (defined as group1/case group) versus control groups comprising healthy individuals, other disease samples (e.g., osteoarthritis), or drug-treatment-related samples (e.g., GSE1919 was split into RA–healthy and RA–OA subsets). Reintegration of multi-group datasets was performed to ensure consistent two-group comparative design (Supplementary Files M5, M6; Tables S2–S4). Final processed data—including raw datasets, normalized expression/methylation matrices (log2(TPM+1) for RNA-seq; RMA-normalized microarray intensities; beta values for methylation arrays), gene-level expression/methylation profiling, and GWAS summary statistics—were compiled and stored in the RABC resource (Supplementary Figure S3; Supplementary Files M2–M4, Table S1).
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis was performed using the R package 'clusterProfiler' (version not explicitly specified, but cited as reference 26). The analysis was applied to differentially expressed genes (DEGs) and differentially methylated genes (DMGs) identified via limma. Default parameters of clusterProfiler were used unless otherwise stated: gene identifiers were mapped to KEGG orthology (KO) terms using the 'bitr' function with 'org.Hs.eg.db' annotation database for human genes; over-representation testing (ORT) was conducted with statistical significance assessed by hypergeometric test; p-values were adjusted for multiple testing using the Benjamini–Hochberg method to control false discovery rate (FDR); pathways with adjusted p-value < 0.05 and q-value < 0.05 were considered significantly enriched; minimum gene count threshold per pathway was set to 2 (default); the 'enrichKEGG' function was employed with 'pvalueCutoff = 0.05', 'qvalueCutoff = 0.05', and 'use_internal_data = TRUE' (leveraging built-in KEGG pathway database within clusterProfiler). Results were integrated into the RABC resource.

Significantly enriched KEGG pathways were filtered and visualized as bubble diagrams, where bubble size represented gene count and color intensity reflected the significance level (−log10(p-value)). sample_description: The study utilized transcriptome data from 1,037 non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) patients and 108 normal tissue samples obtained from The Cancer Genome Atlas (TCGA) database; these data included gene expression quantified as FPKM (fragments per kilobase of transcript per million mapped reads), clinicopathological annotations, and overall survival (OS) information. To ensure data quality, samples with incomplete survival information were excluded, and FPKM values were converted to TPM (transcripts per kilobase million). Four independent validation cohorts—GSE50081, GSE68465, GSE31210, and GSE37745—were retrieved from the Gene Expression Omnibus (GEO) database. Batch effects across these GEO datasets were corrected using the 'combat' function in the 'sva' R package. Additionally, somatic copy number variation (CNV) frequency and mutation data were downloaded from TCGA. The molecular subtyping analysis was performed on two cohorts: TCGA-NSCLC and GSE50081, using mRNA expression profiles of 40 pre-identified necroptosis-related genes (listed in Supplementary Table S1). Differential expression analysis compared cluster A versus cluster B subtypes derived from unsupervised consensus clustering. Prognostic gene screening involved univariate and multivariate Cox regression analyses on NSCLC patients, leading to identification of 89 univariate prognostic genes and subsequently 14 multivariate-significant differentially expressed genes (DEGs) for risk score modeling. Immune and tumor microenvironment analyses incorporated ssGSEA for 23 immune cell types, ESTIMATE for immune and stromal scores, and CIBERSORT for immune deconvolution (though CIBERSORT details were cut off in the provided text).
software_configuration_description: KEGG pathway enrichment analysis was conducted via Gene Set Variation Analysis (GSVA) using the 'GSVA' R package. The gene set collection 'c2.cp.kegg.v7.4.symbols.gmt' was downloaded from the Molecular Signatures Database (MSigDB) and used as the reference pathway annotation file. GSVA was applied to sample-level expression matrices (after TPM normalization and batch correction) to compute pathway activity scores per sample. Statistical significance was determined using a p-value threshold of < 0.05 and a false discovery rate (FDR) cutoff of < 0.05 for identifying significantly enriched KEGG pathways. The resulting GSVA enrichment scores were visualized as a heatmap to facilitate biological interpretation of differential pathway activities across molecular subtypes.

sample_description: The study utilized mRNA sequencing and clinical data from 535 lung adenocarcinoma (LUAD) tumor samples and 59 matched normal lung tissue samples, downloaded from The Cancer Genome Atlas (TCGA) data portal. Immune-related genes (n=1041) were obtained from the ImmPort database (https://www.immport.org/), and metabolism-related genes (n=1613) were retrieved from a published work (reference 19). Low-expressed genes were excluded based on average log2(tpm+1)-normalized expression <0.5. A stringent correlation-based filtering was applied using R's cor.test with Pearson method to identify immune-related metabolic genes: only genes satisfying P<0.05 and absolute correlation coefficient |R|>0.2 were retained, yielding 346 immune-related metabolic genes for downstream analyses. Additionally, 80 independent lung cancer clinical samples with complete survival information were collected for external validation of the prognostic model. Cell line experiments employed four human lung-derived cell lines: BEAS-2B (normal bronchial epithelial), A549, NCI-H292, and Calu-3 (all LUAD-derived); BEAS-2B was sourced from Procell Life Science and Technology Co., Ltd. (Wuhan, China), while A549, NCI-H292, and Calu-3 were obtained from the Cell Bank of the Chinese Academy of Sciences (Shanghai, China). Cells were cultured under standardized conditions: 5% CO2, 37°C, humidified atmosphere, in respective media (DMEM for BEAS-2B; Ham’s F-12K for A549; RPMI-1640 for NCI-H292; MEM for Calu-3), all supplemented with 10% fetal bovine serum (FBS) and antibiotics (100 U/mL penicillin + 100 μg/mL streptomycin). Total RNA was extracted from both clinical tissues and cultured cells using Trizol reagent (Gibco), followed by reverse transcription into cDNA using Takara’s kit and qRT-PCR quantification with SYBR-Green PCR master mix (Roche) on an Applied Biosystems Step One Plus system, using GAPDH as endogenous control and data analyzed via the 2−ΔΔCt method.
software_configuration_description: KEGG enrichment analysis was performed in R using the 'clusterProfiler' (version not specified, but cited as reference 20), 'richplot', and 'ggplot2' (reference 21) packages. The KEGG database was used for systematic functional annotation, pathway mapping, and visualization of gene functions (reference 22). Input genes consisted of the 346 immune-related metabolic differentially expressed genes (DEGs), which were first stratified into up-regulated and down-regulated subsets based on differential expression analysis. Enrichment significance thresholds were set to p-value < 0.05 and q-value (adjusted p-value) < 0.05. For pathway-level interpretation, only KEGG pathways meeting both statistical criteria were considered significantly enriched. Additionally, for metabolic checkpoint identification, KEGG enrichment was re-performed on candidate genes selected via pan-cancer analysis (using TIMER webserver) and cBioPortal-based signaling pathway analysis, applying stricter correlation criteria (Spearman r > 2 or < −2, q-value < 0.05) prior to enrichment.
